Migrations only create what schema specs dictate. This forced: no token table (reset single-use enforced another way, ADR), no `status`/audit cols on `login_session`, `user` is a reserved word → must be quoted. - **Permissions resolve only through a 5-way ACTIVE conjunction** (user·role·permission·`user_role`·`role_permission`) via one JOIN; cached in Redis with **PostgreSQL fallback** — a cache outage never grants access. - **System actor** (`…0001` / `system@platform.internal`) login is **unconditionally rejected** with a generic 401. - **`user_role`/`role_permission` are never hard-deleted** — revocation uses `status`. - **Archive = no silent cascade** (409 `ACADEMIC_DESCENDANTS_EXIST` if active descendants). - **MARKDOWN_LATEX_V1**: only `\(...\)` / `\[...\]` math; `$500` remains text; raw HTML + executable MDX rejected; `isCorrect` never in student payloads; published versions immutable. - **AssessmentQuestion binds an exact QuestionVersion**; latest is never resolved dynamically. - **Frontend token stored in `sessionStorage`** (ADR-0010); httpOnly-cookie is future hardening. - **KaTeX SSR runs at the Next.js boundary**, not Java (no Java KaTeX in frozen stack); Java emits sanitized HTML + math placeholders (ADR/GAP-031). - **Audit persistence deferred to P11 Administration** (`audit_log` owned by frozen `V11`); P1/P2/P3/P4 emit structured `SECURITY` events instead (ADR-0013). - **NODE_ENV**: never run `npm ci`/`next build` with `NODE_ENV=production`; **clear** it (not set to `development`, which `next build` rejects). Ambient `NODE_ENV=production` is a host defect (GAP-019). - **Two test-isolation lessons**: migration tests must assert seed **existence**, not global row counts (other tests add rows); and immutability must be asserted at the **service layer** where the frozen contract exposes no mutation endpoint. - **Migrations numbered by reality** (V4/V5, V6/V7, V8/V9/V10) vs spec-recommended numbers — flagged as gaps (GAP-020/039/045). - **§44 vs §68 tension** (P1): spec §68 lists `SESSION_VIEW` for session list/get while §44 requires student self-scoping — resolved in favour of **§44** (self-scoped; `SESSION_VIEW` widens) — flagged for architecture confirmation (GAP-006). - Everything stays **uncommitted**; assistant has asked whether to commit on `feature/p1-identity` but the user has not answered. ## Errors and feedback - **Frontend tests failing (`React.act is not a function`)** — root cause: ambient `NODE_ENV=production` makes React load `react.production.js`, which does not export `act`. Fixed via `frontend/vitest.config.ts` `test.env.NODE_ENV = "test"`. (A stale `vitest-run.log` "multiple elements found" error was a red herring.) - **`npm ci` under `NODE_ENV=production`** silently drops devDependencies (eslint/vitest/typescript vanish) and hangs `next build`. Fix: clear `NODE_ENV`. - **`next build` rejects `NODE_ENV=development`** — must be unset entirely. - **P0-02/P0-14 verification blocked** — Docker not installed; no compose/nginx/pgbouncer files exist. - **Shell quirks**: no `tail` on this host (use PowerShell); background-task output capture returned empty repeatedly → re-run in foreground; background tasks got reset once mid-`npm ci`. - **Backend optimistic-lock error on asset upload** — pre-setting a generated id made Hibernate treat the entity as detached; fix: use a separate storage key, let Hibernate generate the id. - **Malformed UUID → 500** — added `MethodArgumentTypeMismatchException` → 400 in `GlobalExceptionHandler`. - **P1 migration test isolation bug** — asserted exact global role/permission counts; fixed to assert seed existence. - **P4 test bugs** — (a) never materialized before reading (empty set); (b) immutability asserted against a nonexistent version-mutation endpoint (405) → moved to service layer. - **User course-corrections / feedback patterns**: after each phase the user said only **"document gap(s)"** — a standing instruction that any residual gaps must be written into the register, not left in chat. ## Source

**File:** `D:\smart-exam-portal\specs\04-Database-Schema-V1.5.md` (Version 1.5, Status FROZEN, PostgreSQL, Flyway, UUID PK)

**Delivery module = §29 "Delivery schema", lines 1459–1637.** Table inventory for the domain is fixed at §24 "Delivery" (lines 519–528) and §6 of `specs/14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` (lines 200–211).

**Critical format caveat, stated up front:** §29 uses a compact pseudo-DDL block per table (`<table>` + dashed underline + `column TYPE` lines). It is **not** executable DDL. Across the entire document the token `DEFAULT` never appears, and no Delivery table has a `PK(...)` / `NOT NULL(...)` / `Check:` block. Only these five tables in the whole document carry a `Check:` line: `question_version.review_status` (1035), `assessment_version.engine_type` (1305), `assessment_selection_rule.target_count` (1366), `answer_key_detail.question_outcome` (1688–1690), `background_job.status` (2223). **None of the six Delivery tables has a stated CHECK constraint, NOT NULL list, or column default.**

## 1. Verbatim table definitions (all six quoted exactly)

### 29.1 `assessment_session` (lines 1461–1483)

```sql
assessment_session
------------------
id UUID PK
user_id UUID FK
assessment_schedule_id UUID FK
attempt_id UUID FK
session_status
started_on TIMESTAMPTZ
last_activity_on TIMESTAMPTZ
ended_on TIMESTAMPTZ
```

Indexes.

```text
idx_assessment_session_user_id
idx_assessment_session_schedule_id
idx_assessment_session_session_status
idx_assessment_session_last_activity_on
```

No Unique line. No Check line. Application rule (`14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` lines 249–256):

```text
AssessmentSession is a runtime record.
StudentAttempt is the academic record.
```

### 29.2 `student_attempt` (lines 1485–1519)

Indexes.

```text
idx_student_attempt_user_id
idx_student_attempt_assessment_version_id
idx_student_attempt_assessment_schedule_id
idx_student_attempt_attempt_status
idx_student_attempt_snapshot_status
idx_student_attempt_schedule_snapshot_status
idx_student_attempt_started_on
```

Then, verbatim (line 1519): `The attempt state machine and snapshot state machine are separate.`

No Unique line. No Check line. §55 (lines 2844–2861) mandates two of its indexes "required initially":

```sql
SELECT ...
FROM student_attempt
WHERE user_id = ?
AND assessment_version_id = ?
ORDER BY started_on DESC;
```

```text
idx_student_attempt_user_id
idx_student_attempt_assessment_version_id
```

are required initially. Composite indexes may be added after observing real query patterns.

## 2. Keys, constraints, foreign keys

**Primary keys.** All six: `id UUID PK`. Naming convention §13 (lines 295–303): `Constraint: pk_<table>` → `pk_assessment_session`, `pk_student_attempt`, `pk_attempt_question`, `pk_attempt_question_option`, `pk_student_response`, `pk_delivery_policy`.

**Unique constraints.**

| Table | Stated unique constraints | Absent |
|---|---|---|
| assessment_session | **NONE stated** | no unique at all (incl. no uniqueness on `attempt_id` or on user+schedule) |
| student_attempt | **NONE stated** | no unique at all (incl. no one-active-attempt-per-user/version rule) |
| attempt_question | `UNIQUE(attempt_id, question_version_id)`, `UNIQUE(attempt_id, display_order)` | — |
| attempt_question_option | `UNIQUE(attempt_question_id, question_option_id)`, `UNIQUE(attempt_question_id, display_order)` | — |
| student_response | `UNIQUE(attempt_id, question_version_id)` | — |
| delivery_policy | `UNIQUE(code)` | — |

Naming convention §15: `uk_<table>_<column>` / `uk_<table>_<column1>_<column2>`.

**Foreign keys.** From §37 referential integrity table (lines 2380–2457) — these are the exact parent relationships:

```text
assessment_session         | user_id                 | user
assessment_session         | assessment_schedule_id  | assessment_schedule
assessment_session         | attempt_id              | student_attempt
student_attempt            | user_id                 | user
student_attempt            | assessment_version_id   | assessment_version
student_attempt            | assessment_schedule_id  | assessment_schedule
attempt_question           | attempt_id              | student_attempt
attempt_question           | question_version_id     | question_version
attempt_question_option    | attempt_question_id     | attempt_question
attempt_question_option    | question_option_id      | question_option
student_response           | attempt_id              | student_attempt
student_response           | question_version_id     | question_version
```

`delivery_policy` has **no inbound row in the FK table**; it is a parent only: `assessment_version | delivery_policy_id | delivery_policy` (line 2411).

FK naming §14: `fk_<table>_<reference>`.

**ABSENT on FKs:** no `ON DELETE` / `ON UPDATE` behaviour is specified for any of these foreign keys. §38 Cascading delete (lines 2461–2483) only states the policy in prose — hard cascading deletes are not used for historical examination data, and names `StudentAttempt` among the examples of records that must **not** be cascade-deleted. §39 (line 2491): "A `student_response` cannot reference a nonexistent `student_attempt`."

**Check constraints: NONE for any Delivery table.** (Contrast `delivery_policy`'s implementation, item 9.) §41 (line 2521) is the general position: "The application enforces `is_published = true` only after required validation. A database constraint alone cannot fully validate complex publication rules."

**Nullability: NOT STATED per column.** The only nullability statements that touch these tables are global and indirect:

- §6 Base entity standard (lines 84–95): `id, status, created_by, created_on, updated_by, updated_on`.
- §7 Base entity field standards (lines 99–108) marks all six base fields `Required = Yes` for "Major business tables".
- Consequently: `assessment_session`, `attempt_question` and `attempt_question_option` are **not** base-entity tables and their columns have no required/optional marking at all. The domain model (`02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` §10) adds only a few nullable hints, e.g. `ended_on … Nullable` (664), `snapshot_prepared_on … Nullable`, `started_on … Nullable`, `submitted_on … Nullable`, `total_score … Nullable` (679–682). Those hints exist for `student_attempt`/`assessment_session` only; nothing for `attempt_question`, `attempt_question_option`, `student_response`, `delivery_policy`.
- **No column default is stated anywhere** (the word `DEFAULT` does not occur in the document).

---

## 3. Indexes

The spec lists **index names only** — never an explicit column list. Columns are implied by §16 (lines 332–338): `idx_<table>_<column>`, composite `idx_<table>_<column1>_<column2>`.

| Table | Verbatim index names | Count |
|---|---|---|
| assessment_session | `idx_assessment_session_user_id`, `idx_assessment_session_schedule_id`, `idx_assessment_session_session_status`, `idx_assessment_session_last_activity_on` | 4 |
| student_attempt | `idx_student_attempt_user_id`, `idx_student_attempt_assessment_version_id`, `idx_student_attempt_assessment_schedule_id`, `idx_student_attempt_attempt_status`, `idx_student_attempt_snapshot_status`, `idx_student_attempt_schedule_snapshot_status`, `idx_student_attempt_started_on` | 7 |
| attempt_question | `idx_attempt_question_attempt_id`, `idx_attempt_question_question_version_id`, `idx_attempt_question_attempt_order` | 3 |
| attempt_question_option | `idx_attempt_question_option_attempt_question_id`, `idx_attempt_question_option_question_option_id` | 2 |
| student_response | `idx_student_response_attempt_id`, `idx_student_response_question_version_id` | 2 |
| delivery_policy | **NO Indexes block is given at all** (only the `UNIQUE(code)` constraint, which the DB will back with a unique index) | 0 stated |

Inconsistencies worth flagging (do not resolve by invention):

- `idx_student_attempt_schedule_snapshot_status` implies a **composite** `(assessment_schedule_id, snapshot_status)` but is never described.
- `idx_attempt_question_attempt_order` does not follow `idx_<table>_<column>` cleanly (naming suggests `(attempt_id, display_order)`); never stated.
- §16 example `idx_student_response_attempt_id_question_version_id` is illustrative only and does **not** appear in the §29.5 index list.
- §51 (2794): "Do not add indexes without considering read performance, write overhead, storage, and query pattern." §55 (2861): composite indexes are added later, after observing real query patterns.

**Partial / conditional unique index: ABSENT for all six Delivery tables.** There is **no** `UNIQUE(attempt_id) WHERE superseded_by_id IS NULL`, no `WHERE` clause of any kind on a Delivery object, and no `superseded_by_id` column on `student_attempt`. `superseded_by_id` / `superseded_on` / `superseded_reason` live only on `evaluation` (§30.3, lines 1699–1721), and the document's **single** partial index is on that table, verbatim line 1721:

```
Partial unique index: `UNIQUE(attempt_id) WHERE superseded_by_id IS NULL`.
```

The only other `WHERE` occurrences in the document are inside example DML, not index definitions (line 2240 job-claim `UPDATE`, line 2849 attempt-query `SELECT`).

## 4. Enums / status value sets referenced

§9 "Operational status enums" (lines 132–239), §8 "Status standard" (lines 114–128), §10 "Enum implementation strategy" (lines 243–259).

| Column | Value set | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `assessment_session.session_status` | `ACTIVE`, `EXPIRED`, `LOGGED_OUT` | §9 "Session." (136–140). Same set the domain model assigns explicitly: `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` line 661 `| session_status | Enum | ACTIVE, EXPIRED, LOGGED_OUT |`. §29.1 itself does **not** enumerate it. Note the identical set is also used by `login_session.session_status` (line 759 / 769). |
| `student_attempt.attempt_status` | `NOT_STARTED`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `SUBMITTED`, `EVALUATED` | §9 "Attempt." (151–158); restated verbatim in `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §8 lines 294–301 |
| `student_attempt.snapshot_status` | `PENDING`, `PREPARING`, `READY`, `FAILED` | §9 "Snapshot." (160–167); restated `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §8 lines 303–310 |
| `student_attempt.attempt_mode` | `PRACTICE`, `EXAM` | §9 "Delivery mode." (234–239); declared `VARCHAR(20)` in the table; `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` line 676 `| attempt_mode | VARCHAR(20) | PRACTICE, EXAM |` |
| `student_response.status` | **NO dedicated value set is defined anywhere.** The only applicable statement is §8 standard lifecycle `ACTIVE`, `INACTIVE`, `ARCHIVED` (119–122). `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` line 739 says only `| status | Enum | Lifecycle |` | §7/§8; no response-specific enum |
| `delivery_policy.status` | **Not enumerated in §29.6.** Generic lifecycle `ACTIVE`, `INACTIVE`, `ARCHIVED` applies; domain model line 756 `| status | Enum | Lifecycle |` | §8 |
| `attempt_question` / `attempt_question_option` / `assessment_session` other columns | **no status enum** — these tables carry no `status` column | §29 |

**Derived, NOT stored status/palette values** (do not mistake these for column enums). `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §60 (lines 1506–1521):

```text
NOT_VISITED
VISITED_NOT_ANSWERED
ANSWERED
MARKED_FOR_REVIEW
ANSWERED_AND_MARKED
LOCKED
```

and `08-Exam-Engine-and-CDUI-Contract-V1.5.md` lines 1571–1581 (adds `VISITED`, `UNANSWERED`). Both specs state this palette is *derived from persisted runtime state* / *"an interaction aid, not a source of truth"*, and `14-P5` line 1519: "The frozen response model contains enough fields to represent the common states: visited, marked_for_review, and response."

**Type representation:** §10 (257): "The database representation may use PostgreSQL enum types or controlled `VARCHAR` values with check constraints. The choice remains consistent across the project. Do not allow arbitrary free-text status values." The spec is internally inconsistent on which is used here: it declares `attempt_mode VARCHAR(20)` and `snapshot_status VARCHAR(20)` but leaves `session_status`, `attempt_status` and `status` **untyped** in the column block, and provides **no** CHECK constraint for any of them. The exact DB type is therefore **not specified** for `session_status`, `attempt_status`, `student_response.status`, `delivery_policy.status`.

---

## 5. Immutable snapshot tables and the READY rule

**`attempt_question` and `attempt_question_option` are the immutable runtime snapshot.**

- `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` §10.2 (634–635) verbatim: `| AttemptQuestion | Immutable question snapshot for an attempt |`, `| AttemptQuestionOption | Immutable option ordering snapshot |`.
- §29.3 line 1551 verbatim: `Rows are immutable after snapshot READY.`
- §73 Frozen database rules, item 30 (line 3166) verbatim: `Attempt questions and options are immutable after snapshot READY.`
- `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` line 713 verbatim: `Once the snapshot is READY, AttemptQuestion rows are immutable. Runtime randomization does not mutate them.`
- Note: §29.4 places **no** immutability sentence on `attempt_question_option` itself — only "Preserves the exact option ordering presented to the student." (1578). The immutable-by-READY rule is stated once, on 29.3, and generalized only in §73 rule 30.
- Note the schema has **no mechanism** stated to enforce immutability (no trigger, no rule, no lock column) — it is a stated rule, not a stated constraint. No `updated_by`/`updated_on` exists on `attempt_question` (only `created_on`) and **no timestamp column at all** exists on `attempt_question_option`.

**Snapshot state lives on `student_attempt`, not a separate table.** §35 (line 2245) verbatim: `Snapshot preparation is not stored here. Snapshot state lives on `student_attempt`.` `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §6 (211–213) verbatim: "No new persistent table is required for snapshot preparation. / Snapshot state is tracked on the StudentAttempt record."

**READY gate for entering the exam** — `02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` lines 687–694:

```text
attempt_status  = NOT_STARTED
snapshot_status = READY
access          = VALID
schedule        = OPEN
```

Related: `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` line 495 `4.  attempt_mode is immutable after preparation.`; line 788: "A successful attempt has exactly the expected number of AttemptQuestion rows and expected option rows before `snapshot_status = READY`."; line 727: "If any required step fails, the transaction rolls back and snapshot_status becomes FAILED. A retry is idempotent and does not create duplicates." §73 rule 27 (3163): `Sessions are separate from attempts.`

`delivery_policy`, `assessment_session` and `student_response` are **not** part of the immutable snapshot.

---

## 6. ETag / versioning / stale-write columns

**There is no `response_version` column — in the schema or anywhere.** The schema document contains no `version` column on any Delivery table; the only `version` columns in the document are on versioned *content/assessment/evaluation* entities (`answer_key.version`, `evaluation.version`, etc.).

§29.5 line 1608 is the schema's entire statement, verbatim:

> Stale-write protection uses HTTP conditional requests at the API boundary.

The columns that actually carry the ETag are the **audit timestamps**, per the sibling specs:

- `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` line 1209: `ETag: "resp-{updatedOnMillis}-{crc32OfResponse}"` (identical in `05-API-Contract-V1.5.md` 1487 and `03-Technical-Design-Document-V1.5.md` 1108).
- `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` line 1223: `| If-Match matches current ETag | Accept, increment updated_on, return new ETag |`; line 1224: `| If-Match does not match | 412 Precondition Failed with STALE_RESPONSE |`.
- `03-Technical-Design-Document-V1.5.md` line 1126: "On 412, the client re-fetches the current response, merges state, and retries with the new ETag. An older delayed request never overwrites a newer accepted response."

So: **`student_response.updated_on`** (and `updated_by`, `created_on`, `created_by`) is the stale-write discriminator, plus `student_attempt.updated_on`/`updated_by`. `attempt_question` has `created_on` only; `attempt_question_option` has **no** timestamp column; `assessment_session` has **no** audit columns (only `started_on`, `last_activity_on`, `ended_on`). `AGENTS.md` line 33 confirms the platform rule: "Stale-write protection uses HTTP ETag and If-Match."

**Explicitly forbidden by the freeze** — `14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §155 "No schema invention rule" (line 3073) verbatim:

> An implementation does not add a snapshot_job table, add a response_version column, add a timer_event table, add a section_timer table, add an enrollment table, add a delivery_policy_id column, or add a heartbeat endpoint merely because implementation would feel easier.

---

## 7. Mandated seed / reference data

§59 "Seed data" (lines 2927–2935) verbatim:

> Some system master data may be inserted through Flyway.
>
> Examples: roles, permissions, system lookup values, question templates, supported languages, default delivery policy, system actor.
>
> Seed data is idempotent or protected by appropriate uniqueness rules.

`07-Development-Roadmap-V1.5.md` line 1268 repeats the list: "…default lookup values, default delivery policy, initial question templates, and the system actor."

**ABSENT:** no `code` value, `name`, or `configuration` JSON for the default delivery policy is given anywhere in the spec set (the JSON in §29.6 is labelled "Configuration example", not seed data). Whether the default policy is *mandated* as a row is phrased as "Examples:" / "may be inserted", i.e. **not** an explicit requirement with values. §41/§42 do, however, make `delivery_policy_id present` a precondition of assessment publication (§42, line 2544).

## 8. Recommended Flyway migration files

§57 "Migration structure" (lines 2880–2911), verbatim relevant entries:

```text
V6__create_delivery_tables.sql
V12__create_indexes.sql
V13__insert_initial_master_data.sql
```

- **The Delivery tables are recommended for `V6__create_delivery_tables.sql`** (§57 line 2892). Path root: `db/migration/`.
- Indexes are additionally recommended for a consolidated `V12__create_indexes.sql`; seed/master data for `V13__insert_initial_master_data.sql`.
- §57 also states verbatim (line 2911): `Actual migration numbering may differ.` §58 (2917): `Never edit an already-applied production migration.`

**Repository reality (NOT spec 04 — flagged as implementation evidence, not the frozen spec):** the migration set in this repo does **not** implement `V6__create_delivery_tables.sql`; the delivery tables are absent so far, and only `delivery_policy` exists, in a differently-numbered/renamed file:

- `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V8__create_delivery_policy.sql` — the only Delivery-owned table created. Its header cites the spec directly: "It is created here, with its exact frozen columns (specs/04-Database-Schema-V1.5.md §29.6)".
- No migration exists yet for `assessment_session`, `student_attempt`, `attempt_question`, `attempt_question_option`, `student_response` (glob for `**/*create_delivery*.sql` returns only that V8 file; `V1__create_base_schema.sql` creates no business tables and only `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;`).
- No PostgreSQL enum types are created by V1, so the enum strategy in practice is VARCHAR + CHECK.

For completeness, `V8__create_delivery_policy.sql` resolves several spec absences **for `delivery_policy` only** (lines 11–24):

```sql
CREATE TABLE delivery_policy (
    id            UUID         NOT NULL,
    code          VARCHAR(50)  NOT NULL,
    name          VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
    configuration JSONB,
    status        VARCHAR(20)  NOT NULL,
    created_by    UUID,
    created_on    TIMESTAMPTZ,
    updated_by    UUID,
    updated_on    TIMESTAMPTZ,
    CONSTRAINT pk_delivery_policy PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT uk_delivery_policy_code UNIQUE (code),
    CONSTRAINT ck_delivery_policy_status CHECK (status IN ('ACTIVE', 'INACTIVE', 'ARCHIVED'))
);
```

Note this implementation makes `created_by`/`created_on`/`updated_by`/`updated_on` nullable, which is looser than §7's "Required = Yes" for base-entity fields.

---

## Explicit ABSENCE register (so nothing is assumed)

1. **No NOT NULL list** for any of the six Delivery tables (spec-wide: no `NOT NULL(...)` block exists outside the §25.1/§25.2 examples for `organization` and `user`, lines 610–614, 648–655).
2. **No column DEFAULTs** anywhere in the document (token `DEFAULT` never occurs).
3. **No CHECK constraints** on any Delivery table — no attempt_status/snapshot_status/session_status/status value constraint is specified.
4. **No full Postgres DDL / CREATE TABLE / DDL types** for Delivery tables (only the compact pseudo-DDL blocks). `attempt_status`, `session_status`, `student_response.status`, `delivery_policy.status` have **no SQL type given at all**.
5. **No UNIQUE constraint on `assessment_session` or `student_attempt`** — not even for `assessment_session.attempt_id`, despite the domain model's `AssessmentSession 1:1 StudentAttempt` (`02-Domain-Model-V1.5.md` line 644).
6. **No partial/conditional unique index on any Delivery table**; no `WHERE`-clause index; no superseded-attempt mechanism (`superseded_by_id` exists only on `evaluation`).
7. **No explicit index column lists** — only names; two names (`idx_student_attempt_schedule_snapshot_status`, `idx_attempt_question_attempt_order`) are ambiguous about their columns.
8. **No `ON DELETE`/`ON UPDATE` referential actions** for any FK; only the prose policy "Do NOT cascade delete" for historical data including `StudentAttempt` (§38).
9. **No database-level immutability enforcement** (no trigger/rule) for `attempt_question`/`attempt_question_option` — the READY immutability rule is prose only.
10. **No `response_version` column** — and adding one is explicitly forbidden (`14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md` §155). The ETag is derived from `updated_on`.
11. **No response-status enum values** in spec 04; no delivery-policy status enum enumerated in §29.6; no default-delivery-policy code/values mandated with any specificity.
12. **No partitioning** required: §52 (2814) "Partitioning is not required by default" for the large Delivery tables.
13. **No `organization_id`** on any Delivery table (§62: single organization per deployment; `organization_id` retained "where the domain model requires organizational ownership" — Delivery does not list one).

## Source

`D:\smart-exam-portal\specs\05-API-Contract-V1.5.md` (Version 1.5, Status: FROZEN). Delivery material lives in **§44 Delivery API** (lines 1295–1404), **§45 Attempt API** (1407–1474), **§46 Response API** (1476–1573), plus cross-references in §17/18/19, §62 inventory (2449–2470), §63 error catalogue (2586–2604), §64 authorization matrix (2608–2629), §65 async job contract (2633–2669), §68 performance boundaries (2711–2725).

# 1. Global rules that apply to all Delivery endpoints (verbatim)

Base path (§3): "All APIs are rooted at `/api/v1`."

Auth header (§5):
```http
Authorization: Bearer <JWT>
```

Success envelope (§7):
```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": "Request processed successfully",
  "data": {}
}
```

Error envelope (§12):
```json
{
  "success": false,
  "message": "Validation failed",
  "errors": [
    {
      "field": "email",
      "code": "INVALID_EMAIL",
      "message": "Email address is invalid"
    }
  ],
  "timestamp": "2026-10-07T10:00:00Z",
  "path": "/api/v1/users"
}
```

Error categories (§13): `VALIDATION_ERROR, AUTHENTICATION_ERROR, AUTHORIZATION_ERROR, NOT_FOUND, CONFLICT, BUSINESS_ERROR, INVALID_STATE, RATE_LIMITED, SYSTEM_ERROR`

HTTP status standards (§14, verbatim rows relevant here):
```text
| 200 | Successful retrieval or update |
| 201 | Resource created |
| 202 | Accepted for async processing |
| 204 | Successful operation without response body |
| 400 | Invalid request |
| 401 | Authentication required or invalid |
| 403 | Insufficient permission |
| 404 | Resource not found |
| 409 | Conflict or invalid state |
| 412 | Precondition failed |
| 422 | Business or semantic validation failure |
| 429 | Rate limited |
| 500 | Unexpected server error |
| 503 | Dependency unavailable |
```

Idempotency (§17):
> "Idempotency is required for operations where duplicate requests can create duplicate business effects.
> Important examples.
> ```text
> Payment callbacks
> Attempt submission
> Response save
> Notification delivery
> Background job execution
> Report request
> ```
> The exact implementation uses an idempotency key where required."

Idempotency header (§18):
```http
Idempotency-Key: <unique-client-key>
```
> "The server detects duplicate processing and returns the original logical result where appropriate."

Request correlation (§19):
```http
X-Request-Id: <uuid>
```
> "Every request should carry or receive a request identifier. … The identifier is echoed in the response and appears in structured logs and audit context where available."

**Important:** the contract never attaches `Idempotency-Key` or `X-Request-Id` to any *named* Delivery endpoint — they are stated as global/applicable-operation headers only. Likewise it never gives a required permission string for any Delivery endpoint (see §7 below).

Conditional requests are a design principle (§2, item 16): "HTTP conditional requests for stale-write protection on response save." Frozen (§70, item 21): "HTTP conditional requests for stale-write protection on response save."

## 44.1 Prepare attempts for schedule

**Method / path**
```http
POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/prepare-attempts
```

**Path param:** `scheduleId`. **Request body:** *not specified* — the section contains no request schema.

**Success response**
```http
202 Accepted
```
```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": "Attempt preparation queued",
  "data": {
    "jobId": "<uuid>",
    "status": "QUEUED",
    "scheduleId": "<uuid>"
  }
}
```

**Stated behaviour (verbatim):** "Restricted to authorized operational roles. The operation is idempotent."

No error responses specified for this endpoint. No permission string given ("authorized operational roles" only).

## 44.2 Start assessment

**Method / path**
```http
POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/start
```

**Path param:** `scheduleId`. **Request body:** *not specified* (no body documented). **Headers:** only the global `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>`; no `Idempotency-Key`, no `ETag`/`If-Match` documented for this endpoint.

**Stated validation behaviour (verbatim):**
> "The server validates authentication, authorization, AccessGrant validity, schedule window, attempt eligibility, assessment status, attempt existence, attempt status NOT_STARTED, and snapshot status READY."
> "Start Exam must not create AttemptQuestion or AttemptQuestionOption records."

**Success response** (status code not printed in this section — only the body; by §14 the success/retrieval case would be 200, but the contract does not state a code here):
```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": "Assessment started",
  "data": {
    "sessionId": "<uuid>",
    "attemptId": "<uuid>",
    "assessmentVersionId": "<uuid>",
    "mode": "EXAM",
    "startedOn": "2026-10-10T10:00:00Z",
    "serverTime": "2026-10-10T10:00:00.184Z",
    "timer": {
      "mode": "GLOBAL",
      "durationSeconds": 3600
    },
    "deliveryConfiguration": {}
  }
}
```

**Snapshot not ready — error response (verbatim)**
```http
409 Conflict
```
```json
{
  "success": false,
  "message": "Assessment preparation is not complete",
  "errors": [
    {
      "field": "snapshotStatus",
      "code": "PREPARATION_NOT_READY",
      "message": "Please retry when the prepared attempt is ready"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Error catalogue entry (§63, verbatim):** `| PREPARATION_NOT_READY | 409 | Attempt snapshot not yet READY |`

No other errors (e.g. 403 for access grant, 409 INVALID_STATE for attempt already STARTED) are spelled out as responses for this endpoint, though §63 defines `INVALID_STATE | 409 | State transition is not permitted`. No permission string given.

## 46.2 Save response (optimistic concurrency)

**Method / path / headers (verbatim)**
```http
PUT /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}
If-Match: "<etag>"
Content-Type: application/json
```

**Request body (verbatim)**
```json
{
  "response": "B",
  "timeSpentSeconds": 32,
  "markedForReview": false,
  "visited": true
}
```

**Save behaviour table (verbatim)**

| Condition | Result |
|---|---|
| If-Match matches current ETag | Accept, return new ETag |
| If-Match does not match | 412 Precondition Failed |
| If-Match: * | Accept only if row exists |
| No If-Match | Accept (first save) |

**Precondition failed — error response (verbatim)**
```json
{
  "success": false,
  "message": "Response was modified by another request",
  "errors": [
    {
      "code": "STALE_RESPONSE",
      "message": "The stored response is newer than your request."
    }
  ]
}
```
**Error catalogue entry (§63, verbatim):** `| STALE_RESPONSE | 412 | If-Match precondition failed |`

**Success response body/status:** not specified beyond "Accept, return new ETag" (i.e. a new `ETag` response header; no JSON body or status code printed for the success case).

---

GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses
```
**Stated behaviour (verbatim):** "Used for recovery and review."
**Response schema:** not specified. **Errors:** not specified.

```text

That is 14 endpoints. This list exactly matches the 14 endpoints in §44/§45/§46 above — **no extra Delivery endpoint exists in the inventory and none is missing from the prose sections.**

# 4. Behaviour statements requested (verbatim consolidation)

**Start exam**
- "The server validates authentication, authorization, AccessGrant validity, schedule window, attempt eligibility, assessment status, attempt existence, attempt status NOT_STARTED, and snapshot status READY." (§44.2)
- "Start Exam must not create AttemptQuestion or AttemptQuestionOption records." (§44.2)
- "The architecture requires Start Exam to remain lightweight and to perform no snapshot child-row creation." (§68)

**Prepared attempt / snapshot readiness**
- "Returns the prepared attempt snapshot." (§45.3)
- `POST …/{scheduleId}/prepare-attempts` → "Attempt preparation queued", `data.status: "QUEUED"` with `jobId` (§44.1)
- `PREPARATION_NOT_READY | 409 | Attempt snapshot not yet READY` (§63)
- "Attempt snapshot preparation" is listed under applicable async jobs ("Large reports / Imports / Exports / Analytics recalculation / Large notifications / Scorecard generation / Attempt snapshot preparation"), with job statuses `QUEUED, RUNNING, COMPLETED, COMPLETED_WITH_ERRORS, FAILED, CANCELLED` (§65).

**Response save with optimistic concurrency**
- ETag format `ETag: "resp-{updatedOnMillis}-{crc32OfResponse}"` plus `Cache-Control: no-store` on GET (§46.1).
- Full If-Match behaviour table, 412 on mismatch, `STALE_RESPONSE` body (§46.2).
- Frozen principle: "HTTP conditional requests for stale-write protection on response save." (§70 item 21)

**Submission idempotency**
- "Submission is idempotent." (§46.4)
- "Attempt submission" listed as an operation where "Idempotency is required" (§17), using `Idempotency-Key: <unique-client-key>` (§18).

**Recovery**
- `GET /api/v1/sessions/active` "Used for reconnect and recovery. Does not create a new session or attempt." (§44.3)
- `POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/recover` "Recovery does not reconstruct the attempt snapshot." (§44.5)
- `GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses` "Used for recovery and review." (§46.3)

**Prepare-attempts trigger**
- Triggered by `POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/prepare-attempts`; returns `202 Accepted` with a `jobId`; "Restricted to authorized operational roles. The operation is idempotent." (§44.1)
- Async job pattern (§65): "POST operation → 202 Accepted → jobId → GET job status"; **no Delivery-specific job-status path is given in the contract** (the only job-status path documented anywhere is `GET /api/v1/reports/jobs/{jobId}`-style under §58.2 "Report job status" — Delivery does not state one).

**End session**
- "Ending a session does not automatically submit the attempt unless a configured rule explicitly requires it." (§44.6)

**Critical runtime APIs / optimization (§68, verbatim)**
```text
POST /assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/start
GET  /attempts/{attemptId}
GET  /attempts/{attemptId}/questions
PUT  /attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}
POST /attempts/{attemptId}/submit
```
"These are optimized separately from administrative APIs."

**Rate limiting (§67, verbatim):** "Assessment response APIs are designed so that rate limiting does not interfere with legitimate examination traffic."

**Security boundary (§66, verbatim):** "Students never receive. - Correct answers - Internal scoring rules - Other students' responses … - Raw asset file paths". Also §31: "`isCorrect` is accepted only by authorized content and reviewer APIs. It is never returned by student delivery APIs."

---

# 5. Delivery permissions

**The contract states no Delivery permission codes at all.** §64 "Authorization matrix summary" gives only:

| Module | Typical access |
|---|---|
| Delivery | Student and authorized operations |

followed by: "Exact permission mappings are controlled by RBAC."

The only verbatim access statements inside the Delivery sections are qualitative:
- §44.1 — "Restricted to authorized operational roles."
- §45.1 — "Students see only their own attempts unless authorized staff access the endpoint."

For comparison, the only concrete permission strings that appear anywhere in this document are `QUESTION_VIEW`, `QUESTION_CREATE` (§22.2 example), `EVALUATION_VIEW_HISTORY` (§49.4), `SYSTEM_HEALTH_VIEW` (§56/§64), and `USER_VIEW_PII` (§57) — **none of them Delivery-related**. So there are no Delivery permission codes to list; any such codes would have to come from another spec (e.g. `specs/14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md`), not this contract.

---

# 6. Endpoints referenced but not fully specified (explicit gaps)

These are named in the contract/inventory but have **no documented request schema, response schema, or status codes** — do not invent them:

1. **`POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/prepare-attempts`** — no request body; no error responses.
2. **`POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/start`** — no request body; success HTTP code not printed (body only); only the 409 `PREPARATION_NOT_READY` error is given.
3. **`GET /api/v1/sessions/active`** — no response schema, no codes.
4. **`GET /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}`** — no response schema, no codes.
5. **`POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/recover`** — no request/response schema, no codes.
6. **`POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/end`** — no request/response schema, no codes.
7. **`GET /api/v1/attempts`** — filters and visibility rule only; no response schema, no codes (only implicit §8 collection envelope + §9 `page`/`size`/`sort`).
8. **`GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}`** — "Returns runtime state" only; no schema, no codes.
9. **`GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions`** — example body only; no status codes, no error codes.
10. **`GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions/{questionVersionId}`** — behaviour sentence only; no schema, no codes.
11. **`GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}`** — ETag/`Cache-Control` headers only; no body schema, no codes.
12. **`PUT /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}`** — request body and the 412 `STALE_RESPONSE` path are specified; the **success response body and its HTTP status code are not stated**.
13. **`GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses`** — purpose only; no schema, no codes.
14. **`POST /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/submit`** — request body and success body are specified; success HTTP status code not stated; no error responses documented (notably no explicit 409 `INVALID_STATE` for already-submitted attempts, even though §63 defines that code).

Also unspecified at contract level: any `Idempotency-Key` requirement bound to a specific Delivery endpoint (only the general §17/§18 statements), and any Delivery job-status polling endpoint for `jobId` returned by `prepare-attempts` (only the generic §65 pattern "GET job status").

447: ## 13. Delivery runtime state model
448: 
449: Runtime state.
450: 
451: ```text
452:                  snapshot
453:                     |
454:                     v
455:               NOT_STARTED
456:                     |
457:           Start Exam activation
458:                     |
459:                     v
460:                 IN_PROGRESS
461:                  /          \
462:               submit        expiry
463:                 |              |
464:                 v              v
465:                 SUBMITTED
466:                     |
467:                evaluation
468:                     |
469:                     v
470:                 EVALUATED
471: ```
472: 
473: Session state runs independently.
474: 
475: ```text
476: ACTIVE
477:   |
478:   +-- recover  -> ACTIVE
479:   +-- logout   -> LOGGED_OUT
480:   +-- timeout  -> EXPIRED
481: ```
482: 
483: The end of an AssessmentSession does not silently convert to successful attempt submission unless an approved business rule explicitly requires it.
484: 
485: ---
486: 
487: ## 14. Runtime invariants
488: 
489: The following invariants are mandatory.
490: 
491: ```text
492: 1.  A runtime attempt belongs to exactly one user.
493: 2.  A runtime attempt points to one exact AssessmentVersion.
494: 3.  A runtime attempt points to one exact AssessmentSchedule.
495: 4.  attempt_mode is immutable after preparation.
496: 5.  AttemptQuestion is immutable after snapshot preparation.
497: 6.  AttemptQuestionOption is immutable after snapshot preparation.
498: 7.  StudentResponse can only reference a QuestionVersion assigned to the attempt.
499: 8.  StudentResponse cannot be created for a submitted or evaluated attempt.
500: 9.  Correct answers are never returned by student-facing Delivery APIs.
501: 10. The browser clock is not authoritative.
502: 11. Start Exam does not create snapshot rows.
503: 12. Submit is idempotent.
504: 13. Recovery does not reconstruct a snapshot.
505: 14. Published P4 artifacts are never modified by Delivery.
506: 15. Access authorization is checked server-side.
507: 16. Delivery does not call another module's repositories directly.
508: 17. PostgreSQL is authoritative for persisted runtime state.
509: 18. Redis failure cannot corrupt an attempt.
510: 19. Duplicate active attempts are prevented by application rules.
511: 20. All important state transitions are auditable and observable.
512: 21. Stale-write protection uses HTTP conditional requests.
513: 22. System actor identity is used for background processes.
514: ```
515: 
516: ---
517: 
518: ## 15. Delivery module package boundary
519: 
520: Recommended backend structure.
521: 
522: ```text
523: delivery/
524:   api/
525:     DeliveryController
526:     SessionController
527:     AttemptController
528:     ResponseController
529:     SubmissionController
530:   application/
531:     StartAssessmentService
532:     SessionRecoveryService
533:     AttemptQueryService
534:     ResponseSaveService
535:     SubmissionService
536:     SnapshotPreparationService
537:     AttemptEligibilityService
538:     RuntimeConfigurationService
539:     DeliveryPolicyService
540:   domain/
541:     AssessmentSession
542:     StudentAttempt
543:     AttemptQuestion
544:     AttemptQuestionOption
545:     StudentResponse
546:     DeliveryPolicy
547:   repository/
548:   dto/
549:   mapper/
550:   validation/
551:   exception/
552:   engine/
553:   worker/
554: ```
555: 
556: Cross-module calls use explicit application contracts.
557: 
558: ```text
559: AssessmentContextProvider
560: QuestionSnapshotProvider
561: AccessAuthorizationProvider
562: ExamEngineRegistry
563: EvaluationTrigger
564: ```
565: 
566: These are contracts, not direct repository access.
567: 
568: ---
569: 
570: ## 16. P5 to P4 integration contract
571: 
572: Delivery consumes only the published handoff.
573: 
574: ```text
575: AssessmentVersion
576: BlueprintVersion
577: AssessmentQuestion
578: Exact QuestionVersion IDs
579: QuestionOptions
580: Section
581: Marks
582: Negative marks
583: Duration
584: Schedule mode
585: Engine type
586: Delivery policy
587: Engine-compatible configuration
588: ```
589: 
590: Delivery does not:
591: 
592: ```text
593: Edit AssessmentQuestion
594: Edit AssessmentVersion
595: Resolve latest QuestionVersion
596: Change marks
597: Change negative marks
598: Change question ordering
599: Change BlueprintVersion
600: ```
601: 
602: The preparation worker reads the published definition and materializes the runtime snapshot.
603: 
604: ---
605: 
606: ## 17. P5 to P3 Content contract
607: 
608: The runtime content contract is QuestionVersion, question content, question type, options, assets, and explanation or hint only when runtime policy permits.
609: 
610: Student runtime content uses MARKDOWN_LATEX_V1, supports `\( ... \)`, supports `\[ ... \]`, rejects raw HTML, rejects executable MDX, resolves `[asset: uuid]` through backend authorization, and renders math through the approved KaTeX server-side rendering pipeline.
611: 
612: P5 does not expose asset file paths, server storage paths, QuestionOption.is_correct, private author metadata, review metadata, or answer-key internals.
613: 
614: ---
615: 
616: ## 18. P5 to P1 security contract
617: 
618: Every runtime endpoint requires JWT authentication, user identity resolution, RBAC and permission enforcement, resource ownership authorization, input validation, and structured security logging where applicable.
619: 
620: Student authorization is based on the authenticated user and target attempt or session, not on a client-provided role.
621: 
622: Frontend route protection is convenience only. Backend authorization is authoritative.
623: 
624: ---
625: 
626: ## 19. Access and eligibility boundary
627: 
628: Start Exam verifies.
629: 
630: ```text
631: Authenticated user
632: Schedule exists
633: Schedule is active and open
634: Current time is within allowed window
635: Assessment version is published
636: User has required access
637: Eligible attempt exists
638: Attempt belongs to authenticated user
639: attempt_mode matches schedule mode
640: snapshot_status = READY
641: attempt_status = NOT_STARTED
642: ```
643: 
644: AccessGrant is the authorization source of truth for paid content.
645: 
646: The candidate population mechanism for scheduled mass exam preparation is a P5 design decision addressed during implementation. The chosen mechanism uses existing AccessGrant and operational candidate set. The design is documented before production use.
647: 
648: ---
649: 
650: ## 20. Snapshot preparation architecture
651: 
652: Snapshot preparation is an asynchronous workload.
653: 
654: ```text
655: Schedule
656:    |
657:    v
658: Preparation trigger
659:    |
660:    v
661: Candidate resolver
662:    |
663:    v
664: StudentAttempt PENDING
665:    |
666:    v
667: Claim -> PREPARING
668:    |
669:    v
670: Read immutable AssessmentVersion
671:    |
672:    v
673: Read exact AssessmentQuestion rows
674:    |
675:    v
676: Read exact QuestionVersions and Options
677:    |
678:    v
679: Materialize AttemptQuestion
680:    |
681:    v
682: Materialize AttemptQuestionOption
683:    |
684:    v
685: Mark snapshot READY
686: ```
687: 
688: No snapshot preparation occurs inside the public Start Exam request.
689: 
690: ---
691: 
692: ## 21. Snapshot preparation transaction model
693: 
694: Each attempt is prepared atomically.
695: 
696: Preferred unit.
697: 
698: ```text
699: One StudentAttempt
700: +
701: All AttemptQuestion rows
702: +
703: All AttemptQuestionOption rows
704: +
705: READY transition
706: ```
707: 
708: within one database transaction.
709: 
710: Recommended sequence.
711: 
712: ```text
713: 1.  Claim StudentAttempt PENDING -> PREPARING using conditional update.
714: 2.  Load exact published AssessmentVersion.
715: 3.  Load ordered AssessmentQuestion rows.
716: 4.  Validate QuestionVersion and Option integrity.
717: 5.  Apply permitted pre-start randomization.
718: 6.  Insert AttemptQuestion rows in deterministic order.
719: 7.  Insert AttemptQuestionOption rows in deterministic order.
720: 8.  Update StudentAttempt:
721:       snapshot_status = READY
722:       snapshot_prepared_on = now()
723:       updated_on = now()
724: 9.  Commit.
725: ```
726: 
727: If any required step fails, the transaction rolls back and snapshot_status becomes FAILED. A retry is idempotent and does not create duplicates.
728: 
729: ---
730: 
731: ## 22. Snapshot claiming and worker concurrency
732: 
733: Workers use database-safe claiming.
734: 
735: The worker never selects many PENDING rows and independently assumes ownership without a claim protocol.
736: 
737: Preferred pattern.
738: 
739: ```sql
740: UPDATE student_attempt
741: SET snapshot_status = 'PREPARING',
742:     updated_on = now()
743: WHERE id = :attemptId
744:   AND snapshot_status = 'PENDING';
745: ```
746: 
747: Only the transaction receiving one affected row owns the preparation.
748: 
749: For batches, the implementation may use a bounded claim loop. The final SQL shape is verified against PostgreSQL execution plans and the worker query pattern.
750: 
751: Do not rely on an in-process Java lock for correctness in a multi-instance deployment.
752: 
753: ---
754: 
755: ## 23. Snapshot retry and recovery
756: 
757: The worker tolerates process crash, pod restart, database transient failure, network failure, partial batch failure, and temporary question-content failure.
758: 
759: State handling.
760: 
761: ```text
762: PENDING    -> eligible for first claim
763: PREPARING  -> owned by a worker
764: READY      -> immutable runtime snapshot is usable
765: FAILED     -> retryable only through an explicit retry policy
766: ```
767: 
768: Stale PREPARING detection uses approved existing timestamps and worker operational metadata rather than silently adding a new column.
769: 
770: `updated_on` may participate in stale detection. The exact timeout is an operational setting and is load-tested.
771: 
772: ---
773: 
774: ## 24. Snapshot idempotency rules
775: 
776: The following database constraints are part of the correctness model.
777: 
778: ```text
779: UNIQUE(attempt_id, question_version_id)
780: UNIQUE(attempt_id, display_order)
781: 
782: UNIQUE(attempt_question_id, question_option_id)
783: UNIQUE(attempt_question_id, display_order)
784: ```
785: 
786: The service treats duplicate-key outcomes during a retry as a signal to inspect existing snapshot state, not as permission to append another copy.
787: 
788: A successful attempt has exactly the expected number of AttemptQuestion rows and expected option rows before `snapshot_status = READY`.
789: 
790: ---
791: 
792: ## 25. Snapshot determinism
793: 
794: For the same prepared attempt, the runtime always sees the same QuestionVersion IDs, question order, section, marks, negative marks, and option order.
795: 
796: Randomization, if the assessment configuration permits it, occurs only during preparation. No randomization occurs on question GET, response save, recovery, or submit.
797: 
798: Historical reproducibility is therefore preserved by the snapshot itself.
799: 
800: ---
801: 
802: ## 26. Start Exam - performance-critical path
803: 
804: The Start Exam path is deliberately small.
805: 
806: ```text
807: Authenticate
808:    |
809:    v
810: Authorize
811:    |
812:    v
813: Resolve schedule
814:    |
815:    v
816: Resolve candidate attempt
817:    |
818:    v
819: Read READY snapshot metadata
820:    |
821:    v
822: Resolve ExamEngine
823:    |
824:    v
825: Resolve normalized DeliveryConfiguration
826:    |
827:    v
828: Transition NOT_STARTED -> IN_PROGRESS
829:    |
830:    v
831: Create or activate lightweight AssessmentSession
832:    |
833:    v
834: Return runtime context
835: ```
836: 
837: Start Exam does not insert AttemptQuestion rows, insert AttemptQuestionOption rows, randomize questions, bulk-load the question bank, construct a fresh snapshot, call evaluation, or rebuild the entire assessment definition.
838: 
839: This is the main defense against a thundering-herd failure at exam start.
840: 
841: ---
842: 
843: ## 27. Start Exam concurrency
844: 
845: For one attempt, concurrent Start requests result in exactly one logical start.
846: 
847: Preferred invariant.
848: 
849: ```text
850: attempt_status = NOT_STARTED
851:             |
852:             v conditional transition
853: attempt_status = IN_PROGRESS
854: ```
855: 
856: Use a conditional update or row-level transaction lock scoped to the StudentAttempt.
857: 
858: Example logical condition.
859: 
860: ```text
861: UPDATE only when:
862: attempt_status = NOT_STARTED
863: AND snapshot_status = READY
864: ```
865: 
866: A second request sees the already-started state and returns the established attempt and session context or a deterministic invalid-state response according to the approved idempotency policy.
867: 
868: Never use a global lock on an assessment or schedule.
869: 
870: ---
871: 
872: ## 28. Start Exam API
873: 
874: ```http
875: POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/start
876: ```
877: 
878: The baseline source response includes.
879: 
880: ```json
881: {
882:   "success": true,
883:   "message": "Assessment started",
884:   "data": {
885:     "sessionId": "<uuid>",
886:     "attemptId": "<uuid>",
887:     "assessmentVersionId": "<uuid>",
888:     "startedOn": "2026-10-10T10:00:00Z",
889:     "durationMinutes": 60
890:   }
891: }
892: ```
893: 
894: The ExamEngine and CDUI contract additionally requires normalized runtime configuration to be returned.
895: 
896: A complete P5 response may include.
897: 
898: ```json
899: {
900:   "success": true,
901:   "message": "Assessment started",
902:   "data": {
903:     "sessionId": "<uuid>",
904:     "attemptId": "<uuid>",
905:     "assessmentVersionId": "<uuid>",
906:     "mode": "EXAM",
907:     "startedOn": "2026-10-10T10:00:00Z",
908:     "serverTime": "2026-10-10T10:00:01Z",
909:     "timer": {
910:       "mode": "GLOBAL",
911:       "durationSeconds": 3600
912:     },
913:     "deliveryConfiguration": {}
914:   }
915: }
916: ```
917: 
918: The exact OpenAPI shape is aligned with the ExamEngine and CDUI contract before implementation freeze.
919: 
920: ---
921: 
922: ## 29. Start Exam - snapshot not ready
923: 
924: If `snapshot_status != READY`, return.
925: 
926: ```http
927: 409 Conflict
928: ```
929: 
930: with error code.
931: 
932: ```text
933: PREPARATION_NOT_READY
934: ```
935: 
936: The frontend displays a recoverable preparation state and does not repeatedly hammer Start Exam.
937: 
938: The backend does not fall back to synchronous generation.
939: 
940: ---
941: 
942: ## 30. Attempt creation
943: 
944: P5 creates the StudentAttempt runtime record as part of preparation, not from the Start Exam request.
945: 
946: An attempt is created with.
947: 
948: ```text
949: attempt_status  = NOT_STARTED
950: snapshot_status = PENDING
951: attempt_mode    = schedule.mode
952: assessment_schedule_id = exact schedule
953: assessment_version_id  = exact published version
954: ```
955: 
956: The attempt is bound to the authenticated student or candidate before snapshot preparation.
957: 
958: ---
959: 
960: ## 31. Practice mode
961: 
962: `PRACTICE` and `EXAM` are immutable attempt modes.
963: 
964: Practice may permit flexible navigation, explanation visibility, hint visibility, no enforced duration where configured, and repeated practice according to assessment rules.
965: 
966: The server determines the mode. The frontend never permits `PRACTICE -> EXAM` or `EXAM -> PRACTICE` within the same attempt.
967: 
968: Practice still uses the same snapshot integrity model so that runtime correctness is not a second implementation.
969: 
970: ---
971: 
972: ## 32. Exam mode
973: 
974: `EXAM` is the strict delivery mode.
975: 
976: The runtime enforces the approved navigation policy, timer policy, submission policy, validation policy, and presentation capability contract.
977: 
978: Any action that violates the normalized policy is rejected by the backend even if the UI hides the control.
979: 
980: ---
981: 
982: ## 33. ExamEngine resolution
983: 
984: P5 integrates the approved interface.
985: 
986: ```java
987: public interface ExamEngine {
988:   ExamEngineType type();
989:   DeliveryConfiguration resolveConfiguration(
990:       AssessmentContext context,
991:       StudentAttemptContext attemptContext
992:   );
993:   NavigationPolicy navigationPolicy(...);
994:   TimerPolicy timerPolicy(...);
995:   SubmissionPolicy submissionPolicy(...);
996:   ValidationPolicy validationPolicy(...);
997: }
998: ```
999: 
1000: Engine resolution is registry-based.
1001: 
1002: ```text
1003: ExamEngineRegistry
1004:       |
1005:       v
1006: ExamEngineType
READspecs\14-P5-Delivery-V1.5.md
Read 1/1 file, 500 lines
Note: showing lines 1007-1506. More of the file remains. Use offset=1507 to continue.
1007:       |
1008:       v
1009: Concrete ExamEngine
1010: ```
1011: 
1012: Supported types.
1013: 
1014: ```text
1015: TCS_ION
1016: IBPS
1017: NTA
1018: UPSC
1019: RAILWAY
1020: CUSTOM
1021: ```
1022: 
1023: The frontend does not branch on these names.
1024: 
1025: ---
1026: 
1027: ## 34. Normalized DeliveryConfiguration
1028: 
1029: The runtime configuration is declarative.
1030: 
1031: Conceptual groups.
1032: 
1033: ```text
1034: Capabilities
1035: Navigation
1036: Timer
1037: Submission
1038: Validation
1039: Presentation
1040: ```
1041: 
1042: The JSON is an experience and enforcement contract, not executable configuration.
1043: 
1044: ---
1045: 
1046: ## 35. Delivery configuration security
1047: 
1048: Configuration never contains `renderScript`, `javascript`, `customComponent`, `eval`, `expression`, `databaseQuery`, or `arbitraryUrl`.
1049: 
1050: The frontend uses a typed Zod schema.
1051: 
1052: The backend validates the resolved configuration before returning it.
1053: 
1054: Unsupported configuration fails safely rather than being silently interpreted.
1055: 
1056: ---
1057: 
1058: ## 36. Navigation state machine
1059: 
1060: Navigation is controlled by the backend policy.
1061: 
1062: Conceptual modes.
1063: 
1064: ```text
1065: FREE
1066: SEQUENTIAL
1067: SECTION_SEQUENTIAL
1068: CUSTOM
1069: ```
1070: 
1071: A navigation request is validated against attempt status, session status, attempt question membership, current section, target question order, engine navigation policy, and section switch capability.
1072: 
1073: The backend does not trust a question index sent by the browser without resolving it against the immutable AttemptQuestion snapshot.
1074: 
1075: ---
1076: 
1077: ## 37. Question identity in runtime
1078: 
1079: The source contract uses `QuestionVersion ID` in the response API.
1080: 
1081: The delivery design additionally exposes.
1082: 
1083: ```text
1084: attemptQuestionId
1085: questionVersionId
1086: displayOrder
1087: sectionName
1088: marks
1089: negativeMarks
1090: option ordering
1091: ```
1092: 
1093: The backend resolves `attemptId + questionVersionId` to the immutable AttemptQuestion. This prevents a malicious client from selecting a QuestionVersion that was never assigned to the attempt.
1094: 
1095: ---
1096: 
1097: ## 38. Get attempt
1098: 
1099: ```http
1100: GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}
1101: ```
1102: 
1103: Returns only student-safe runtime state.
1104: 
1105: Recommended logical information.
1106: 
1107: ```text
1108: attemptId
1109: assessmentVersionId
1110: assessment or schedule identifiers where safe
1111: attemptMode
1112: attemptStatus
1113: snapshotStatus
1114: startedOn
1115: submittedOn
1116: serverTime
1117: deadline or remaining timer representation
1118: deliveryConfiguration
1119: progress summary
1120: ```
1121: 
1122: Never returns correct answers, answer key internals, private authoring data, private audit data, other students, payment secrets, or server file paths.
1123: 
1124: The endpoint verifies attempt ownership or authorized staff permission.
1125: 
1126: ---
1127: 
1128: ## 39. Get attempt questions
1129: 
1130: ```http
1131: GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions
1132: ```
1133: 
1134: The result is built from the prepared snapshot, not from the live AssessmentQuestion table.
1135: 
1136: Expected runtime data.
1137: 
1138: ```text
1139: attemptQuestionId
1140: questionVersionId
1141: displayOrder
1142: sectionName
1143: marks
1144: negativeMarks
1145: question content
1146: question type
1147: authorized options
1148: authorized assets
1149: ```
1150: 
1151: The backend preserves snapshot order.
1152: 
1153: The list supports efficient retrieval and does not load the entire QuestionBank.
1154: 
1155: ---
1156: 
1157: ## 40. Get attempt question
1158: 
1159: ```http
1160: GET /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions/{questionVersionId}
1161: ```
1162: 
1163: The backend verifies attempt belongs to user, attempt is active, and QuestionVersion is assigned to attempt.
1164: 
1165: The endpoint never resolves the current or latest version of the Question. It resolves the exact QuestionVersion captured by AttemptQuestion.
1166: 
1167: ---
1168: 
1169: ## 41. Question payload protection
1170: 
1171: Student runtime APIs omit `QuestionOption.is_correct`, answer key, review notes, author identity, question health score, internal taxonomy metadata not required by UI, asset file path, and database identifiers not part of the contract.
1172: 
1173: Only the information required to render and answer the question is exposed.
1174: 
1175: ---
1176: 
1177: ## 42. Autosave architecture
1178: 
1179: ```http
1180: PUT /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}
1181: ```
1182: 
1183: Baseline request.
1184: 
1185: ```json
1186: {
1187:   "response": "B",
1188:   "timeSpentSeconds": 32,
1189:   "markedForReview": false,
1190:   "visited": true
1191: }
1192: ```
1193: 
1194: The operation is idempotent.
1195: 
1196: Autosave is an intentional API interaction with debounce and throttle, request cancellation where safe, bounded retry, offline queue handling for short network interruptions, server acknowledgement, and duplicate protection.
1197: 
1198: The browser does not assume a response is persisted until the server acknowledges success.
1199: 
1200: ---
1201: 
1202: ## 43. Stale-write protection contract
1203: 
1204: The save endpoint uses HTTP conditional requests.
1205: 
1206: GET returns.
1207: 
1208: ```text
1209: ETag: "resp-{updatedOnMillis}-{crc32OfResponse}"
1210: Cache-Control: no-store
1211: ```
1212: 
1213: PUT accepts.
1214: 
1215: ```http
1216: If-Match: "<etag>"
1217: ```
1218: 
1219: Rules.
1220: 
1221: | Condition | Behaviour |
1222: |---|---|
1223: | If-Match matches current ETag | Accept, increment updated_on, return new ETag |
1224: | If-Match does not match | 412 Precondition Failed with STALE_RESPONSE |
1225: | If-Match: * | Accept only if row exists, otherwise 412 |
1226: | No If-Match | Accept (first save). Server records ETag |
1227: 
1228: On 412, the client re-fetches the current response, merges local state, and retries with the new ETag.
1229: 
1230: The important invariant.
1231: 
1232: > An older delayed request never silently overwrites a newer accepted response.
1233: 
1234: ---
1235: 
1236: ## 44. Response save transaction
1237: 
1238: Preferred server-side flow.
1239: 
1240: ```text
1241: Authenticate
1242:    |
1243:    v
1244: Authorize attempt
1245:    |
1246:    v
1247: Validate active session and attempt state
1248:    |
1249:    v
1250: Resolve AttemptQuestion
1251:    |
1252:    v
1253: Validate response against question template
1254:    |
1255:    v
1256: Validate If-Match precondition where provided
1257:    |
1258:    v
1259: Normalize response representation
1260:    |
1261:    v
1262: UPSERT student_response
1263:    |
1264:    v
1265: Update session last_activity_on
1266:    |
1267:    v
1268: Commit
1269: ```
1270: 
1271: The persistence operation does not create a new row when one already exists for the same attempt and question.
1272: 
1273: ---
1274: 
1275: ## 45. Response validation
1276: 
1277: Before persistence, validate attempt belongs to user, attempt_status is IN_PROGRESS, session is valid, question is assigned, question version belongs to snapshot, response conforms to QuestionTemplate, response is within permitted size, timeSpentSeconds is non-negative, markedForReview is permitted, and visited is consistent with runtime state.
1278: 
1279: A client cannot use response content to select an unassigned option.
1280: 
1281: The exact option-response encoding remains governed by the question-template content contract.
1282: 
1283: ---
1284: 
1285: ## 46. Time tracking
1286: 
1287: `student_response.time_spent_seconds` is the frozen persistence field.
1288: 
1289: The browser may estimate elapsed time for UX, but server-side validation prevents obviously invalid values.
1290: 
1291: The runtime tracks question timing as a client measurement that is persisted on response saves. The official attempt deadline remains server authoritative.
1292: 
1293: For analytics, the persisted question time supports derived question time, section time, assessment time, topic time, and student average time.
1294: 
1295: P5 does not create a new timing event table.
1296: 
1297: ---
1298: 
1299: ## 47. Idempotency model
1300: 
1301: Idempotency is mandatory for response save, submission, payment callbacks, notification delivery, and background jobs.
1302: 
1303: For Delivery.
1304: 
1305: ```text
1306: Same logical mutation
1307:       |
1308:       v
1309: Same effective result
1310:       |
1311:       v
1312: No duplicate business effect
1313: ```
1314: 
1315: `X-Request-Id` remains the correlation identifier.
1316: 
1317: Where an operation requires deduplication beyond the domain unique keys, the exact persistence and short-lived coordination mechanism is selected during implementation.
1318: 
1319: ---
1320: 
1321: ## 48. Session heartbeat and activity
1322: 
1323: The frozen schema includes `last_activity_on`.
1324: 
1325: The session updates this timestamp on meaningful runtime activity.
1326: 
1327: Do not update it on every UI render.
1328: 
1329: Meaningful activity.
1330: 
1331: ```text
1332: Question load
1333: Response save
1334: Navigation action
1335: Recovery
1336: Submission
1337: ```
1338: 
1339: No heartbeat endpoint is added because the frozen API contract does not include one.
1340: 
1341: ---
1342: 
1343: ## 49. Get active session
1344: 
1345: ```http
1346: GET /api/v1/sessions/active
1347: ```
1348: 
1349: Purpose: reconnect and recovery.
1350: 
1351: The endpoint returns the currently recoverable session for the authenticated student.
1352: 
1353: It does not create a new session, create a new attempt, rebuild snapshot, change attempt mode, or restart the timer.
1354: 
1355: If no recoverable session exists, return the standard not-found or valid-state response.
1356: 
1357: ---
1358: 
1359: ## 50. Get session
1360: 
1361: ```http
1362: GET /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}
1363: ```
1364: 
1365: Returns runtime-safe state: session status, attempt id, schedule id, started time, last activity, server time, and effective timer state.
1366: 
1367: Never returns private internal session details that the student does not need.
1368: 
1369: ---
1370: 
1371: ## 51. Session recovery
1372: 
1373: ```http
1374: POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/recover
1375: ```
1376: 
1377: Recovery means.
1378: 
1379: ```text
1380: Authenticate
1381:    |
1382:    v
1383: Verify session ownership
1384:    |
1385:    v
1386: Verify session is still recoverable
1387:    |
1388:    v
1389: Verify attempt state
1390:    |
1391:    v
1392: Re-resolve normalized DeliveryConfiguration
1393:    |
1394:    v
1395: Read persisted attempt state
1396:    |
1397:    v
1398: Return current runtime context
1399: ```
1400: 
1401: Recovery never reconstructs AttemptQuestion rows. Recovery never resets `started_on`. Recovery never restarts the timer.
1402: 
1403: ---
1404: 
1405: ## 52. Recovery after process restart
1406: 
1407: The system recovers correctly after API instance restart, frontend refresh, browser crash, temporary network loss, load balancer rebalance, and Redis restart.
1408: 
1409: The recovery source of truth is PostgreSQL, not React state, Redis-only session state, or browser localStorage alone.
1410: 
1411: Local UI state may improve UX but is never treated as authoritative.
1412: 
1413: ---
1414: 
1415: ## 53. Redis failure behavior
1416: 
1417: If Redis is unavailable, core attempt reads continue, response persistence continues, submission continues, and PostgreSQL remains authoritative.
1418: 
1419: Redis may provide cache, short-lived coordination, rate limiting support, and non-critical optimization, but P5 does not require Redis availability to preserve exam correctness.
1420: 
1421: ---
1422: 
1423: ## 54. Timer authority
1424: 
1425: The server is authoritative.
1426: 
1427: At start, the client receives serverTime, startedOn, duration, and effective timer policy.
1428: 
1429: For a global timer.
1430: 
1431: ```text
1432: deadline = startedOn + effective duration
1433: ```
1434: 
1435: The browser renders a countdown.
1436: 
1437: The backend recomputes deadline validity on response save, navigation, question load, recovery, submission, and other protected runtime actions.
1438: 
1439: A browser cannot extend an attempt by changing local clock values.
1440: 
1441: ---
1442: 
1443: ## 55. Timer drift model
1444: 
1445: Client timer behavior uses serverTime as the authoritative reference, clientClock as a display aid, and periodic server interactions as synchronization opportunities.
1446: 
1447: The client tolerates tab suspension, sleep and wake, clock changes, CPU throttling, and network latency.
1448: 
1449: When a server response indicates less time remains, the UI immediately resynchronizes.
1450: 
1451: ---
1452: 
1453: ## 56. Timer expiry
1454: 
1455: When an important request arrives after the deadline, non-submission state-changing actions are rejected.
1456: 
1457: For configured auto-submit behavior.
1458: 
1459: ```text
1460: Expire
1461:    |
1462:    v
1463: Server submission path
1464:    |
1465:    v
1466: Finalize attempt once
1467:    |
1468:    v
1469: Mark SUBMITTED
1470: ```
1471: 
1472: Timer expiry is race-safe with manual submission. Only one finalization wins logically.
1473: 
1474: ---
1475: 
1476: ## 57. Global timer
1477: 
1478: The global timer is fully expressible with the frozen runtime fields: `started_on` and assessment duration.
1479: 
1480: For the pilot, this is the baseline timer implementation. A global deadline remains stable across refresh and recovery.
1481: 
1482: ---
1483: 
1484: ## 58. Section-wise timer
1485: 
1486: Section-wise timer semantics require careful persistence design.
1487: 
1488: The pilot may use only section-timing behavior that can be proven correct from the frozen persistence model. A separate schema and contract amendment is required before enabling behavior that requires new persisted state.
1489: 
1490: The implementation does not silently add a timer event table.
1491: 
1492: ---
1493: 
1494: ## 59. Navigation and section switching
1495: 
1496: The normalized configuration controls back navigation, section switching, question review, clear response, and question palette.
1497: 
1498: The frontend uses these capabilities to render controls.
1499: 
1500: The backend validates every protected transition.
1501: 
1502: An exam name such as IBPS or NTA never appears in React navigation logic.
1503: 
1504: ---

1506: ## 60. Question palette state
1507: 
1508: The question palette is derived from persisted runtime state.
1509: 
1510: ```text
1511: NOT_VISITED
1512: VISITED_NOT_ANSWERED
1513: ANSWERED
1514: MARKED_FOR_REVIEW
1515: ANSWERED_AND_MARKED
1516: LOCKED
1517: ```
1518: 
1519: The frozen response model contains enough fields to represent the common states: visited, marked_for_review, and response.
1520: 
1521: The palette remains a presentation of server-backed state, not an independent source of truth.
1522: 
1523: ---
1524: 
1525: ## 61. Clear response
1526: 
1527: A clear-response action uses the existing response mutation contract rather than introducing another persistence model.
1528: 
1529: A clear operation results in a deterministic response state representing no answer, visited according to runtime semantics, and mark-for-review according to current UI state.
1530: 
1531: ---
1532: 
1533: ## 62. Mark for review
1534: 
1535: The frozen API allows `markedForReview` inside the response payload.
1536: 
1537: For V1 implementation, PUT response is the preferred path because the source response model already carries the state.
1538: 
1539: Do not create both paths without a specific need.
1540: 
1541: ---
1542: 
1543: ## 63. Submission API
1544: 
1545: ```http
1546: POST /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/submit
1547: ```
1548: 
1549: Baseline request.
1550: 
1551: ```json
1552: {
1553:   "confirmed": true
1554: }
1555: ```
1556: 
1557: Server flow.
1558: 
1559: ```text
1560: Authenticate
1561:    |
1562:    v
1563: Authorize
1564:    |
1565:    v
1566: Validate session
1567:    |
1568:    v
1569: Validate attempt
1570:    |
1571:    v
1572: Validate deadline
1573:    |
1574:    v
1575: Finalize persisted response state
1576:    |
1577:    v
1578: Transition attempt to SUBMITTED
1579:    |
1580:    v
1581: Stamp submitted_on
1582:    |
1583:    v
1584: End or transition session as appropriate
1585:    |
1586:    v
1587: Trigger evaluation
1588: ```
1589: 
1590: Submission is a state transition, not a client-side cleanup operation.
1591: 
1592: ---
1593: 
1594: ## 64. Submission idempotency
1595: 
1596: Two or more simultaneous submit requests produce one logical submission.
1597: 
1598: Valid outcomes.
1599: 
1600: ```text
1601: First request transitions IN_PROGRESS -> SUBMITTED
1602: Later identical request returns the same submitted state
1603: ```
1604: 
1605: Invalid outcomes.
1606: 
1607: ```text
1608: Duplicate evaluation triggers
1609: Different submitted timestamps
1610: Resurrection to IN_PROGRESS
1611: Double session end
1612: ```
1613: 
1614: The implementation uses transaction-safe state transition logic.
1615: 
1616: ---
1617: 
1618: ## 65. Submit versus session end
1619: 
1620: The frozen API contains.
1621: 
1622: ```http
1623: POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/end
1624: ```
1625: 
1626: and separately.
1627: 
1628: ```http
1629: POST /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/submit
1630: ```
1631: 
1632: Therefore.
1633: 
1634: ```text
1635: End session is not the same as Submit attempt
1636: ```
1637: 
1638: Unless a formally approved delivery rule says otherwise.
1639: 
1640: A support or operator session termination does not accidentally produce an official submission.
1641: 
1642: ---
1643: 
1644: ## 66. Submission race
1645: 
1646: A submission can race with last autosave, timer expiry, recovery, duplicate submit, and session expiry.
1647: 
1648: Required outcome.
1649: 
1650: ```text
1651: One authoritative terminal attempt state
1652: ```
1653: 
1654: Recommended transaction rule.
1655: 
1656: ```text
1657: Lock or conditionally update StudentAttempt
1658: Verify IN_PROGRESS
1659: Verify current server time
1660: Finalize only once
1661: ```
1662: 
1663: Any background evaluation trigger uses the resulting submitted attempt ID and an idempotent trigger strategy.
1664: 
1665: ---
1666: 
1667: ## 67. Evaluation handoff
1668: 
1669: P5 stops at `StudentAttempt = SUBMITTED`.
1670: 
1671: Then P5 hands off to Evaluation.
1672: 
1673: Evaluation is the source of official scoring, answer-key application, result, and scorecard.
1674: 
1675: P5 does not compute official marks as part of Delivery. It may compute operational counts such as answered count, visited count, and review count for runtime UX only.
1676: 
1677: ---
1678: 
1679: ## 68. Submission response
1680: 
1681: ```json
1682: {
1683:   "success": true,
1684:   "message": "Assessment submitted successfully",
1685:   "data": {
1686:     "attemptId": "<uuid>",
1687:     "status": "SUBMITTED",
1688:     "submittedOn": "2026-10-10T11:00:00Z"
1689:   }
1690: }
1691: ```
1692: 
1693: The response does not reveal unofficial score data unless a separate approved result API provides it.
1694: 
1695: ---
1696: 
1697: ## 69. Attempt history
1698: 
1699: ```http
1700: GET /api/v1/attempts
1701: ```
1702: 
1703: Filters: assessmentId, assessmentVersionId, status, from, to.
1704: 
1705: Students see only their own attempts. Staff visibility is permission controlled.
1706: 
1707: P5 does not return private attempts outside the caller's authorized organization context.
1708: 
1709: ---
1710: 
1711: ## 70. API inventory - frozen Delivery contract
1712: 
1713: ```http
1714: POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/start
1715: 
1716: GET  /api/v1/sessions/active
1717: GET  /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}

1719: POST /api/v1/sessions/{sessionId}/end
1720: 
1721: GET  /api/v1/attempts
1722: GET  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}
1723: GET  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions
1724: GET  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/questions/{questionVersionId}
1725: 
1726: GET  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}
1727: PUT  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses/{questionVersionId}
1728: GET  /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/responses
1729: 
1730: POST /api/v1/attempts/{attemptId}/submit
1731: 
1732: POST /api/v1/assessment-schedules/{scheduleId}/prepare-attempts
1733: ```
1734: 
1735: No new public endpoint is added merely to make the API aesthetically symmetrical.

3079: ## 156. Runtime anti-patterns
3080: 
3081: Reject snapshot creation during Start, browser-controlled deadlines, frontend authorization, correct answers in payloads, exam-name branching in React, JPA entity exposure, N+1 question loading, global synchronization locks, unbounded retry loops, Redis-only attempt state, synchronous heavy evaluation on submit, cross-module repository access, and long database transactions around network calls.
3082: 
3083: ---

3086: 
3087: Execute one task at a time with checkpoints.
3088:

3090: P5-01  Create Delivery module skeleton
3091: P5-02  Create runtime domain entities
3092: P5-03  Create repositories and projections
3093: P5-04  Implement DeliveryPolicy contract
3094: P5-05  Implement ExamEngine registry integration
3095: P5-06  Implement DeliveryConfiguration validation

3097: P5-08  Implement attempt creation
3098: P5-09  Implement snapshot claim
3099: P5-10  Implement snapshot preparation
3100: P5-11  Implement snapshot retry and reconciliation
3101: P5-12  Implement snapshot integrity checks
3102: P5-13  Implement Start Exam
3103: P5-14  Implement session create and activate
3104: P5-15  Implement attempt read APIs
3105: P5-16  Implement question read APIs
3106: P5-17  Implement response validation and save with ETag
3107: P5-18  Implement stale-write precondition handling
3108: P5-19  Implement recovery

3111: P5-22  Implement submission

3119: P5-30  Produce P5 release evidence
3120: ```

3166: ## 161. P5-13 to P5-23 acceptance
3167:
