12: /**
13:  * Parses and validates Blueprint JSONB configuration into the typed model
14:  * ({@code specs/13-P4-Assessment-V1.5.md §11, §85}).
15:  *
16:  * <p>The parsing pipeline is JSONB -&gt; typed deserialization -&gt; field validation -&gt; cross-field
17:  * validation. The platform ObjectMapper rejects unknown fields, so uncontrolled or executable
18:  * configuration never reaches business logic.
19:  */
20: @Component
21: public class BlueprintConfigurationParser {
22: 
23:     private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
24: 
25:     public BlueprintConfigurationParser(ObjectMapper objectMapper) {
26:         this.objectMapper = objectMapper;
27:     }
28: 
29:     /** Parses configuration, failing with a controlled error on malformed or unknown content. */
30:     public BlueprintConfiguration parse(String configuration) {
31:         if (configuration == null || configuration.isBlank()) {
32:             return null;
33:         }
34:         try {
35:             return objectMapper.readValue(configuration, BlueprintConfiguration.class);
36:         } catch (Exception invalid) {
37:             throw new ValidationException("Blueprint configuration is invalid",
38:                     List.of(ApiError.of("configuration", "BLUEPRINT_INVALID",
39:                             "Configuration is not valid, or contains unsupported or executable fields")));
40:         }
41:     }
42: 
43:     /**
44:      * Validates a parsed configuration for publication ({@code §12-§18, §85}).
45:      *
46:      * <p>Structural rules: at least one section; each section has a non-blank name, a unique
47:      * ascending display order, a positive question count, non-negative marks, and non-negative
48:      * duration.
49:      */
50:     public void validate(BlueprintConfiguration configuration) {
51:         List<ApiError> errors = new ArrayList<>();
52:         if (configuration == null || configuration.sections() == null || configuration.sections().isEmpty()) {
53:             errors.add(ApiError.of("configuration", "BLUEPRINT_INVALID", "At least one section is required"));
54:             throw new ValidationException("Blueprint configuration is invalid", errors);
55:         }
56:         Set<Integer> orders = new HashSet<>();
57:         Set<String> names = new HashSet<>();
58:         for (BlueprintConfiguration.Section section : configuration.sections()) {
59:             if (section.name() == null || section.name().isBlank()) {
60:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_NAME_INVALID", "Section name is required"));
61:             } else if (!names.add(section.name())) {
62:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_NAME_DUPLICATE", "Section names must be unique"));
63:             }
64:             if (section.displayOrder() == null || section.displayOrder() <= 0) {
65:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_ORDER_INVALID", "Section displayOrder must be positive"));
66:             } else if (!orders.add(section.displayOrder())) {
67:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_ORDER_DUPLICATE", "Section displayOrder must be unique"));
68:             }
69:             if (section.questionCount() == null || section.questionCount() <= 0) {
70:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_COUNT_INVALID", "Section questionCount must be positive"));
71:             }
72:             if (section.marks() != null && section.marks().signum() < 0) {
73:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_MARKS_INVALID", "Section marks must not be negative"));
74:             }
75:             if (section.durationMinutes() != null && section.durationMinutes() < 0) {
76:                 errors.add(ApiError.of("sections", "SECTION_DURATION_INVALID",
77:                         "Section durationMinutes must not be negative"));
78:             }
79:         }
80:         if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
81:             throw new ValidationException("Blueprint configuration is invalid", errors);
82:         }
83:     }
84: }
Thinking
I'll simplify DeliveryPolicyParser to deserialize into a typed record with the platform ObjectMapper (tools.jackson). The platform ObjectMapper rejects unknown fields (fail-on-unknown-properties: true). But the delivery policy JSON has known keys; my DeliveryPolicyAttributes record matches them (allowBackNavigation, allowSectionSwitch, autoSaveIntervalSeconds, allowQuestionReview, allowClearResponse, allowMarkForReview, autoSubmitOnExpiry). But the seeded DEFAULT policy only has 4 keys → deserializing into a record with 7 required fields would fail (missing fields → null/error). With Jackson, missing primitives default to false/0, so it'd work but autoSaveIntervalSeconds would be 0 (not 10). Hmm.

Better: parse into a `Map<String,Object>` and read keys with defaults, plus reject forbidden keys. Using tools.jackson ObjectMapper.readValue(json, Map.class)? Jackson 3 typing: `objectMapper.readValue(json, new TypeReference<Map<String,Object>>(){})`. TypeReference in tools.jackson.core.type.TypeReference.

Simplest robust: use readTree and JsonNode. Jackson 3 JsonNode: `tools.jackson.databind.JsonNode`. `objectMapper.readTree(String)` returns JsonNode. Node API: `node.isObject()`, `node.properties()`? Jackson 3 changed `fields()` to `properties()` (I used properties()). Also `node.get(name)`, `node.isBoolean()`, `node.booleanValue()` (Jackson 3 renamed asBoolean()→booleanValue()? Actually Jackson 3 renamed `asBoolean()` to `booleanValue()` and `asInt()` to `intValue()`). I used booleanValue()/intValue().

Rather than guess the Jackson 3 JsonNode API, safer: deserialize to a typed record with `@JsonIgnoreProperties(ignoreUnknown = false)`? Unknown rejection is global. The concern is missing fields. If I model all 7 fields as `Boolean`/`Integer` (boxed, optional), missing → null, then apply defaults. That's clean and uses the platform ObjectMapper (rejecting unknown/executable keys robustly, better than my hand-rolled check!). Since fail-on-unknown-properties=true, any forbidden key (renderScript, eval, etc.) would throw → fail-safe. 

So rewrite DeliveryPolicyParser:
```java
@Component
public class DeliveryPolicyParser {
  private final ObjectMapper objectMapper;
  public DeliveryPolicyParser(ObjectMapper om){...}
  public DeliveryPolicyAttributes parse(String json) {
    if (json==null||json.isBlank()) return defaults();
    try {
      var raw = objectMapper.readValue(json, RawDeliveryPolicy.class);
      return new DeliveryPolicyAttributes(
        or(raw.allowBackNavigation, true), or(raw.allowSectionSwitch,true),
        raw.autoSaveIntervalSeconds==null?10:raw.autoSaveIntervalSeconds,
        or(raw.allowQuestionReview,true), or(raw.allowClearResponse,true),
        or(raw.allowMarkForReview,true), or(raw.autoSubmitOnExpiry,false));
    } catch (Exception malformedOrForbidden) {
      return DeliveryPolicyAttributes.defaults();
    }
  }
  private static boolean or(Boolean v, boolean d){ return v==null?d:v; }
  @JsonIgnoreProperties(ignoreUnknown = false)  // default; unknown rejected by platform mapper
  record RawDeliveryPolicy(Boolean allowBackNavigation, Boolean allowSectionSwitch, Integer autoSaveIntervalSeconds,
     Boolean allowQuestionReview, Boolean allowClearResponse, Boolean allowMarkForReview, Boolean autoSubmitOnExpiry) {}
}
```
Note: fail-on-unknown-properties=true means unknown → exception → defaults (fail safe). Good; matches §35 "Unsupported configuration fails safely".

Records with Jackson 3: should deserialize fine.

Remove JsonNode/Iterator/Set imports.

Let me rewrite DeliveryPolicyParser.

Jackson 3 — let me rewrite the parser to use the platform tools.jackson ObjectMapper.

```java
public int preparePendingBatch(int limit) {
  List<UUID> pending = attemptRepository.findPendingIds(PENDING, PageRequest.of(0, limit));
  int prepared = 0;
  for (UUID id : pending) {
    Boolean ok = transactionTemplate.execute(status -> prepare(id));
    if (Boolean.TRUE.equals(ok)) prepared++;
  }
  return prepared;
}
```

One more consideration: `markSnapshotFailed` after a materialize exception: inside the template tx, the exception is caught in prepare(), then markSnapshotFailed executes (bulk update) — but if the tx is already marked rollback-only (because a JPA exception occurred), the bulk update may still run but commit will roll back → attempt stays PREPARING. Hmm. Is the tx marked rollback-only when a RuntimeException is thrown by a repository call and caught by user code? Hibernate's exception translation sets rollback-only only when the exception propagates through the transaction interceptor. If caught inside the same method (user code), the tx is NOT automatically marked rollback-only by Spring (that happens at the @Transactional boundary). However Hibernate may mark the Session for rollback (e.g., after a flush failure) — `SessionException` sets the session's rollbackOnly. For constraint violations during flush, the Hibernate session is marked rollback-only. Then the catch's subsequent operations fail.

To be safe, wrap the "mark failed" in a NEW transaction (REQUIRES_NEW). That requires a separate bean or a TransactionTemplate with PROPAGATION_REQUIRES_NEW.

Simplest robust design: 
- `prepare(UUID)` does the claim in its own tx (template NEW default REQUIRED), and materialize in a separate tx; on failure, mark FAILED in a separate tx.

But claim must be its own tx and materialize its own. Let me structure:
```java
public boolean prepare(UUID attemptId) {
  Boolean claimed = transactionTemplate.execute(s -> attemptRepository.claimForPreparation(...) == 1);
  if (!Boolean.TRUE.equals(claimed)) return false;
  try {
    transactionTemplate.executeWithoutResult(s -> { StudentAttempt a = attemptRepository.findById(attemptId).orElseThrow(); materialize(a); attemptRepository.markSnapshotReady(...); });
    return true;
  } catch (RuntimeException failure) {
    log...;
    transactionTemplate.executeWithoutResult(s -> attemptRepository.markSnapshotFailed(...));
    return false;
  }
}
```
Wait `executeWithoutResult` exists on TransactionTemplate? Yes (`executeWithoutResult(Consumer<TransactionStatus>)`).

This gives: claim tx, materialize tx, fail tx — all separate. Clean and robust. And `prepare` is not @Transactional (remove annotation) so it can be called from tests/batch/worker.

READfrontend\lib\api-client.ts
Read 1/1 file, 124 lines
1: /**
2:  * The single centralized API client.
3:  *
4:  * Responsibilities (specs/09-P0-Foundation-V1.5.md §43):
5:  * base URL, request correlation id, authentication token boundary, common headers, and response
6:  * and error normalization. It contains no exam behaviour, scoring, authorization, timer
7:  * authority, or submission rules.
8:  *
9:  * Blind automatic retry is deliberately absent: state-changing requests must never be retried
10:  * implicitly (§46). Any future retry for a specific operation requires idempotency.
11:  */
12: import axios, { isAxiosError, type AxiosAdapter, type AxiosInstance } from "axios";
13: 
14: import { getAccessToken } from "@/lib/auth/token-storage";
15: import { env } from "@/lib/env";
16: import { normalizeApiError, type ApiClientError } from "@/lib/errors";
17: import type { ApiEnvelope, ApiResult } from "@/types/api";
18: 
19: export const REQUEST_ID_HEADER = "X-Request-Id";
20: export const API_VERSION_PREFIX = "/api/v1";
21: 
22: /** Supplies the current bearer token, or null when unauthenticated. */
23: export type TokenProvider = () => string | null;
24: 
25: export interface ApiClientOptions {
26:   baseURL?: string;
27:   tokenProvider?: TokenProvider;
28:   /** Called on a 401 for a non-authentication request (for example, an expired session). */
29:   onUnauthorized?: (error: ApiClientError) => void;
30:   /** Injectable transport, used by tests to avoid real network calls. */
31:   adapter?: AxiosAdapter;
32: }
33: 
34: function newRequestId(): string {
35:   if (typeof crypto !== "undefined" && typeof crypto.randomUUID === "function") {
36:     return crypto.randomUUID();
37:   }
38:   return `req-${Date.now().toString(36)}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}`;
39: }
40: 
41: export function createApiClient(options: ApiClientOptions = {}): AxiosInstance {
42:   const instance = axios.create({
43:     baseURL: options.baseURL ?? `${env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL}${API_VERSION_PREFIX}`,
44:     timeout: 15_000,
45:     headers: { Accept: "application/json" },
46:     ...(options.adapter ? { adapter: options.adapter } : {}),
47:   });
48: 
49:   instance.interceptors.request.use((config) => {
50:     config.headers.set(REQUEST_ID_HEADER, newRequestId());
51: 
52:     const token = options.tokenProvider?.();
53:     if (token) {
54:       config.headers.set("Authorization", `Bearer ${token}`);
55:     }
56:     return config;
57:   });
58: 
59:   instance.interceptors.response.use(
60:     (response) => response,
61:     (error: unknown) => {
62:       const normalized = normalizeApiError(error);
63:       if (normalized.status === 401 && !isAuthenticationRequest(error)) {
64:         options.onUnauthorized?.(normalized);
65:       }
66:       return Promise.reject(normalized);
67:     },
68:   );
69: 
70:   return instance;
71: }
72: 
73: /** Authentication endpoints may legitimately return 401 without meaning the session expired. */
74: function isAuthenticationRequest(error: unknown): boolean {
75:   if (!isAxiosError(error)) {
76:     return false;
77:   }
78:   const url = error.config?.url;
79:   return typeof url === "string" && url.includes("/auth/");
80: }
81: 
82: let unauthorizedHandler: (() => void) | null = null;
83: 
84: /** Registers the single handler invoked when a request is rejected with 401. */
85: export function setUnauthorizedHandler(handler: (() => void) | null): void {
86:   unauthorizedHandler = handler;
87: }
88: 
89: /** Application-wide client. The bearer token is read from the single token store. */
90: export const apiClient = createApiClient({
91:   tokenProvider: getAccessToken,
92:   onUnauthorized: () => unauthorizedHandler?.(),
93: });
94: 
95: function toResult<T>(envelope: ApiEnvelope<T>): ApiResult<T> {
96:   return {
97:     data: envelope.data,
98:     message: envelope.message,
99:     pagination: envelope.pagination,
100:   };
101: }
102: 
103: export async function apiGet<T>(
104:   url: string,
105:   params?: Record<string, unknown>,
106: ): Promise<ApiResult<T>> {
107:   const response = await apiClient.get<ApiEnvelope<T>>(url, { params });
108:   return toResult(response.data);
109: }
110: 
111: export async function apiPost<T>(url: string, body?: unknown): Promise<ApiResult<T>> {
112:   const response = await apiClient.post<ApiEnvelope<T>>(url, body);
113:   return toResult(response.data);
114: }
115: 
116: export async function apiPut<T>(url: string, body?: unknown): Promise<ApiResult<T>> {
117:   const response = await apiClient.put<ApiEnvelope<T>>(url, body);
118:   return toResult(response.data);
119: }
120: 
121: /** Sends a DELETE (archive) request. Archive responses carry no body (HTTP 204). */
122: export async function apiDelete(url: string): Promise<void> {
123:   await apiClient.delete(url);
124: }

8:   assessmentListItemSchema,

23:   return entries.length === 0 ? "" : "?" + entries.map(([k, v]) => `${k}=${encodeURIComponent(String(v))}`).join("&");

```ts
/** Formats an ETag header value into the canonical quoted form used for If-Match. */
export function toIfMatch(etag: string | null | undefined): string | undefined {
  if (!etag) return undefined;
  const bare = etag.replace(/^"|"$/g, "");
  return bare.length === 0 ? undefined : `"${bare}"`;
}
/** Extracts the STALE_RESPONSE precondition code from a normalized API error. */
export function isStaleResponse(body: unknown): boolean {
  const errors = (body as {errors?: Array<{code?: string}>})?.errors;
  return Array.isArray(errors) && errors.some(e => e?.code === "STALE_RESPONSE");
}
/** Remaining seconds, clamped at zero, based on the server deadline. */
export function remainingSeconds(deadlineIso: string | null | undefined, nowMs: number): number | null {
  if (!deadlineIso) return null;
  const deadline = Date.parse(deadlineIso);
  if (Number.isNaN(deadline)) return null;
  return Math.max(0, Math.floor((deadline - nowMs) / 1000));
}
```
These are testable and cover stale-write handling + server-backed timer.

Test file for these.

"use client";
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { formatDuration, remainingSeconds } from "@/features/delivery/lib/runtime-helpers";

export function RuntimeTimer({ deadline, serverTime }: { deadline: string | null; serverTime: string }) {
  const [now, setNow] = useState(() => Date.now());
  useEffect(() => { const id = setInterval(() => setNow(Date.now()), 1000); return () => clearInterval(id); }, []);
  const remaining = remainingSeconds(deadline, now);
  const label = remaining === null ? "Untimed" : formatDuration(remaining);
  const drift = ...; // serverTime not used for display except as sync reference
  return <div role="timer" aria-live="polite" data-testid="runtime-timer">{label}</div>;
}
```

Let me implement autosave with debounce:
```tsx
const save = useSaveResponse(attemptId);
const timers = useRef<Record<string, ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>>({});
function queueSave(question, next) {
  setAnswers(prev => ({...prev, [question.questionVersionId]: {...prev[...], ...next}}));
  const key = question.questionVersionId;
  clearTimeout(timers.current[key]);
  setStatus(prev => ({...prev, [key]: "saving"}));
  timers.current[key] = setTimeout(() => void persist(question, next), saveDelayMs);
}
async function persist(question, next) {
  const key = question.questionVersionId;
  const current = answersRef... 
  try {
    const result = await save.mutateAsync({ questionVersionId: key, body: {...}, ifMatch: etags[key] });
    setEtags(prev => ({...prev, [key]: result.etag}));
    setStatus(...saved);
  } catch (e) {
    if (e instanceof ApiClientError && isStaleResponse(e.body)) {
       const fresh = await getResponse(attemptId, key); // re-fetch
       setEtags(prev=>({...prev,[key]:fresh.etag}));
       // merge: keep local answer, retry once
       ...
    } else setStatus(error)
  }
}
```
This is getting long; I'll keep it correct but compact. Need ApiClientError shape: does it have `.body`? Let me check lib/errors quickly. It has `normalizeApiError` and `ApiClientError`. I need to know if ApiClientError exposes the error envelope (errors[]). Let me read lib/errors.ts.

READfrontend\lib\errors.ts
Read 1/1 file, 100 lines
1: /**
2:  * Error normalization.
3:  *
4:  * Every failure surfaced by the API client becomes a single {@link ApiClientError} type so that
5:  * UI code never has to understand transport details. Internal details are never displayed to the
6:  * user; the correlation identifier is surfaced so a problem can be traced.
7:  */
8: import { isAxiosError } from "axios";
9: import type { ApiErrorEnvelope, ApiErrorItem } from "@/types/api";
10: 
11: export const NETWORK_ERROR_CODE = "NETWORK_ERROR";
12: export const UNKNOWN_ERROR_CODE = "UNKNOWN_ERROR";
13: 
14: export class ApiClientError extends Error {
15:   readonly status: number | null;
16:   readonly code: string;
17:   readonly requestId?: string;
18:   readonly fieldErrors: ApiErrorItem[];
19: 
20:   constructor(params: {
21:     message: string;
22:     status: number | null;
23:     code: string;
24:     requestId?: string;
25:     fieldErrors?: ApiErrorItem[];
26:   }) {
27:     super(params.message);
28:     this.name = "ApiClientError";
29:     this.status = params.status;
30:     this.code = params.code;
31:     this.requestId = params.requestId;
32:     this.fieldErrors = params.fieldErrors ?? [];
33:   }
34: 
35:   /** True when the request never reached the server (offline, DNS, timeout, connection refused). */
36:   static isNetworkError(error: unknown): boolean {
37:     return error instanceof ApiClientError && error.status === null;
38:   }
39: 
40:   static isUnauthorized(error: unknown): boolean {
41:     return error instanceof ApiClientError && error.status === 401;
42:   }
43: 
44:   static isForbidden(error: unknown): boolean {
45:     return error instanceof ApiClientError && error.status === 403;
46:   }
47: }
48: 
49: function isErrorEnvelope(value: unknown): value is ApiErrorEnvelope {
50:   return (
51:     typeof value === "object" &&
52:     value !== null &&
53:     "success" in value &&
54:     (value as { success?: unknown }).success === false
55:   );
56: }
57: 
58: /**
59:  * Converts any thrown value into an {@link ApiClientError}.
60:  *
61:  * Messages come from the server envelope when available; otherwise a safe generic message is
62:  * used. Stack traces and internal details are never propagated.
63:  */
64: export function normalizeApiError(error: unknown): ApiClientError {
65:   if (error instanceof ApiClientError) {
66:     return error;
67:   }
68: 
69:   if (isAxiosError(error)) {
70:     const response = error.response;
71: 
72:     if (!response) {
73:       return new ApiClientError({
74:         message: "Unable to reach the server. Check your connection and try again.",
75:         status: null,
76:         code: NETWORK_ERROR_CODE,
77:       });
78:     }
79: 
80:     const payload: unknown = response.data;
81:     const envelope = isErrorEnvelope(payload) ? payload : undefined;
82:     const requestId =
83:       envelope?.requestId ??
84:       (response.headers?.["x-request-id"] as string | undefined);
85: 
86:     return new ApiClientError({
87:       message: envelope?.message ?? "The request could not be completed.",
88:       status: response.status,
89:       code: envelope?.errors?.[0]?.code ?? "REQUEST_FAILED",
90:       requestId,
91:       fieldErrors: envelope?.errors ?? [],
92:     });
93:   }
94: 
95:   return new ApiClientError({
96:     message: "An unexpected error occurred.",
97:     status: null,
98:     code: UNKNOWN_ERROR_CODE,
99:   });
100: }

My `isStaleResponse(body)` expects `{errors:[{code}]}`. I can call `isStaleResponse({ errors: error.fieldErrors })`. Let me add a small overload-friendly usage. Also `error.status === 412`.

DeliveryMetrics:
```java
package com.company.exam.delivery.observability;
@Component
public class DeliveryMetrics {
  public static final String ATTEMPT_STARTED = "delivery.attempt.started";
  public static final String SNAPSHOT_PREPARED = "delivery.snapshot.prepared";
  public static final String SUBMISSION = "delivery.attempt.submitted";
  public static final String STALE_RESPONSE = "delivery.response.stale";
  private final MeterRegistry registry;
  public DeliveryMetrics(MeterRegistry registry){this.registry=registry;}
  public void attemptStarted(String mode){ registry.counter(ATTEMPT_STARTED,"mode", mode==null?"UNKNOWN":mode).increment(); }
  public void snapshotPrepared(){ registry.counter(SNAPSHOT_PREPARED).increment(); }
  public void submission(){ registry.counter(SUBMISSION).increment(); }
  public void staleResponse(){ registry.counter(STALE_RESPONSE).increment(); }
}
```
MeterRegistry bean exists (prometheus). Good.

Actually simpler: after executeWithoutResult returns, call metrics.snapshotPrepared(). 
- ResponseSaveService: on stale → metrics.staleResponse(). Add param.

First write the metrics test.

Then recompile + run full suite.

DeliveryMetricsTest using SimpleMeterRegistry:
```java
class DeliveryMetricsTest {
  @Test void countersAreRegistered() {
    SimpleMeterRegistry registry = new SimpleMeterRegistry();
    DeliveryMetrics metrics = new DeliveryMetrics(registry);
    metrics.attemptStarted("EXAM"); metrics.snapshotPrepared(); metrics.attemptSubmitted(); metrics.staleResponse();
    assertThat(registry.get("delivery.attempt.started").tag("mode","EXAM").counter().count()).isEqualTo(1.0);
    assertThat(registry.get("delivery.snapshot.prepared").counter().count()).isEqualTo(1.0);
    assertThat(registry.get("delivery.attempt.submitted").counter().count()).isEqualTo(1.0);
    assertThat(registry.get("delivery.response.stale").counter().count()).isEqualTo(1.0);
  }
}
```
SimpleMeterRegistry is in micrometer-core (present via prometheus registry). import io.micrometer.core.instrument.simple.SimpleMeterRegistry.

Let me write and run.

58: | GAP-036 | Authorization | Content permission codes are implementation candidates from `12-P3 §128`; the API contract names only some of them | Some seeded codes may need realignment with the Identity catalog | The permission entity is configuration-driven | Realign with Identity catalog (with GAP-021) | Open |
59: | GAP-037 | Caching | No Redis cache is added for published question content | Published reads hit PostgreSQL each time | `12-P3 §62/§131` make caching optional; correctness-first | Optional hardening when load justifies it | Deferred (ADR-0016) |
60: | GAP-038 | Performance | Content list/detail/publish/render performance (`§172`) and observability (`§147/§149`) are not measured on realistic data | No latency/throughput evidence | Requires staging data | Performance phase / staging | Open |
61: | GAP-039 | Migrations | Content tables are `V6` and seeds `V7`, not the recommended `V4`/`V13` | Numbering differs from the recommended list | Earlier phases used `V2`-`V5`; the schema document says numbering may differ | None (informational) | Resolved / informational (ADR-0016) |
62: | GAP-040 | Delivery boundary | `delivery_policy` (Delivery-owned) is created in migration `V8` ahead of P5 | The Delivery phase inherits an already-created table | The frozen `assessment_version.delivery_policy_id` FK requires it | Delivery phase adopts and owns it | By-design (ADR-0017) |
63: | GAP-041 | Filtering | The assessment list `examId` filter is unsupported: the frozen schema has no path from an assessment to an exam | A supplied `examId` returns a clear 400 rather than filtering | The `assessment` table has no exam column | Revisit only with a schema change | Open |
64: | GAP-042 | Errors | `QUESTION_POOL_SHORTAGE` detail (requested/available/deficit) is conveyed in the message, not as separate fields | Callers parse the numbers from the message text | Platform `ApiError` shape is field/code/message only | Add structured fields only if the envelope changes | By-design |
65: | GAP-043 | API surface | No assessment-version mutation endpoint exists; engine-type immutability is enforced and verified at the service boundary | Engine changes after publication are impossible via the API | The frozen contract has no `PUT` for assessment versions | Add only with contract review | By-design (ADR-0017) |
66: | GAP-044 | Scheduling | Schedule "close" maps to status `INACTIVE`; there is no dedicated `CLOSED` status | A closed schedule is `INACTIVE`; runtime state is derived from status and times | `13-P4 §71` forbids a second persistent status field | Revisit with approval | By-design / informational |
67: | GAP-045 | Migrations | Assessment is `V9`/`V10` (delivery_policy `V8`), not the recommended `V5`/`V104`/`V105` | Numbering differs from the recommended list | Earlier phases used `V2`-`V7`; numbering may differ | None (informational) | Resolved / informational |
68: | GAP-046 | Runtime | P4 contains no Start Exam, snapshot creation, `StudentAttempt`, or `AttemptQuestion` | Runtime execution is delivered by P5 | `13-P4 §128-§130` excludes runtime data | P5 Delivery | By-design (ADR-0017) |
69: | GAP-047 | Selection | The "least-recently-used in prior assessments" tie-break is approximated by question-version id ascending | Ordering is deterministic but not LRU-accurate | Determinism is the frozen requirement (`§46-§47`) | Implement LRU when prior-use tracking is added | Deferred |
70: | GAP-048 | Authorization | Assessment permission codes are `13-P4 §145` candidates | Codes may need realignment with the Identity catalog | Permission entity is configuration-driven | Realign with Identity catalog (with GAP-021/036) | Open |

78: ## 2. Deliberate non-goals (not gaps)
79: 
80: These are out of scope by specification and are listed so they are not mistaken for omissions:
81: 
82: - P0/P1: Organization / user / role / session administration **screens** (the API and
83:   permission-aware UI infrastructure exist; a full admin UI does not).
84: - P0/P1: SSO, OAuth, 2FA, biometric authentication, device trust, multi-tenant authorization.
85: - P1: Password-change endpoint (the contract defines only forgot/reset).
86: - Business modules still unimplemented: Delivery, Evaluation, Analytics, Commerce, Communication,
87:   Administration.
88: - P2: Academic master **bulk import** and other tooling are not in the P2 task list.
89: - P2: Academic data is intentionally not organization-scoped (single-organization deployment;
90:   `04-Schema §62`).
91: - P3: A bulk content-import platform and a Content-specific search engine (Elasticsearch/OpenSearch)
92:   are not implemented; search uses PostgreSQL.
93:

97: P0-12 CI/CD + P0-13 secret scan      -> closes GAP-008, GAP-010, GAP-028

99: P0-11 Observability stack            -> closes GAP-009
100: Hardening phase                      -> closes GAP-005, GAP-011, GAP-012, GAP-013
101: P10 Communication                    -> closes GAP-002
102: P11 Administration                   -> closes GAP-001
103: Architecture confirmation            -> closes GAP-006
104: Identity catalog alignment           -> realigns GAP-021, GAP-036

107: Content caching hardening            -> closes GAP-037 (optional)
108: Delivery / files endpoint            -> closes GAP-034
109: Pilot content provisioning           -> closes GAP-035
110: ```

112: ## 4. Raising a new gap
113: 
114: If an implementation task discovers a missing or contradictory frozen contract:
115: 
116: 1. Stop the affected task.
117: 2. Add an entry here using the same columns.
118: 3. If the resolution would change a frozen contract, open an ADR and route it through the
119:    architecture-change process — do not change the contract locally.

7: Commands were run and their results recorded, not claimed.

10: 
11: | Command | Result |
12: |---|---|
13: | `cd backend && ./mvnw clean verify` | PASS — 165 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, BUILD SUCCESS |
14: | `cd frontend && npm run lint` | PASS — 0 errors |
15: | `npm run type-check` | PASS |
16: | `npm test` | PASS — 47 tests, 16 files |
17: | `npm run build` | PASS — `/blueprints`, `/assessments`, `/assessments/[id]` built |
18: 
19: Assessment backend tests: migration (5) + API/verifications (6) = 11.
20: 
21: ## 2. The three requested verifications
22: 
23: **Rule materialization is deterministic and idempotent** — `AssessmentApiIntegrationTest.ruleMaterializationIsDeterministicAndIdempotent`:
24: - Two assessment versions with the same rule configuration over the same published pool materialize
25:   the **same** question-version set (id-ascending tie-break).
26: - Re-running materialization leaves the set **unchanged** (rules with `materialized_on` are skipped).
27: 
28: **Publication blocks incomplete assessments** — `AssessmentApiIntegrationTest`:
29: - `publicationIsBlockedWhenTheAssessmentIsIncomplete` → `/validate` returns `valid=false` with
30:   `QUESTION_COUNT_MISMATCH`; `/publish` returns **422**.
31: - `publicationIsBlockedWithoutAnEngineType` → `valid=false`, `/publish` **422**.
32: 
33: **Engine type is immutable after publication** — `AssessmentApiIntegrationTest.engineTypeIsImmutableAfterPublication`:
34: - After publish, `AssessmentVersionService.update(...)` with a new engine type throws
35:   `InvalidStateException`; the version still reports `CUSTOM`. (The frozen contract exposes no
36:   version-mutation endpoint, so the guard is at the service boundary.)
37: 
38: ## 3. Golden path and historical reproducibility (P4-200, P4-202)
39: 
40: `theGoldenPathPublishesADeterministicAssessment`: publish a blueprint version → create an assessment
41: version (engine type `CUSTOM`, delivery policy) → materialize rules → validate (`valid=true`) →
42: preview → publish (`published=true`, `totalQuestions=2`). The published version still binds the exact
43: `blueprintVersionId`; re-materialization on the published version returns **409**.
44: 
45: ## 4. Acceptance checklists
46: 
47: `§175-§196`: module skeleton; schema verification (9 tables + delivery_policy, constraints, indexes);
48: blueprint CRUD and versions; typed configuration; blueprint validation and publication; assessment
49: CRUD; assessment versions; assessment questions; selection rules and materialization; validation;
50: preview; publication; scheduling; collections; frontend; security and audit — implemented and covered.
51: 
52: ## 5. Known limitations
53: 
54: See [`docs/known-gaps.md`](../../known-gaps.md). P4-relevant: delivery_policy created ahead of P5
55: (GAP-040), `examId` assessment filter unsupported (GAP-041), shortage detail conveyed in the error
56: message (GAP-042), engine immutability has no API mutation path (GAP-043), schedule close maps to
57: `INACTIVE` (GAP-044), migration numbering V8/V9/V10 (GAP-045), no Start Exam/snapshot by design
58: (GAP-046), LRU tie-break approximated (GAP-047), permission names as candidates (GAP-048), the lean
59: P4 frontend / missing builders (GAP-049), no archive endpoints (GAP-050), blueprint `language` ids not
60: verified (GAP-051), blueprint publication not required before assessment publication (GAP-052), P4
61: performance not measured (GAP-053), no assessment Redis cache (GAP-054). CI is still absent
62: (GAP-008); local verification is green.

176: - The host environment sets `NODE_ENV=production`; `npm ci` must run with `NODE_ENV` unset or
177:   devDependencies are skipped.

179: - No idle session timeout; the frozen schema has no last-activity column (ADR-0011).
180: - Access token is stored in `sessionStorage`; httpOnly-cookie transport is a future hardening item
181:   (ADR-0010).

ADR-0018 content: P5 delivery runtime decisions:
- Async snapshot preparation worker with conditional claim (no in-process lock); student_attempt carries the snapshot state machine (no new table).
- Start Exam is a conditional state transition only (zero snapshot inserts).
- Stale-write protection uses If-Match with an ETag derived from student_response.updated_on + CRC32 (no response_version column).
- Evaluation handoff via background_job (frozen table) using the system actor; triggered only by the winning submit transition.
- Clock: server authoritative; global timer from started_on + duration.
- AccessGrant candidate population deferred; pilot uses the STUDENT-role operational candidate set via a new Identity contract.
- Delivery permission codes seeded (none in the frozen catalogue).

Also document that `background_job` was created in V12 ahead of Infrastructure (GAP-055).

Let me write it.

Then gaps GAP-055..058:
- GAP-055: Migrations — `background_job` (Infrastructure) created in V12 ahead of its phase (prerequisite for eval handoff). By-design.
- GAP-056: Authorization — Delivery permission codes are not in the frozen Identity catalog; minimal operational codes seeded. Open (align with GAP-021/036/048).
- GAP-057: Candidate population — pilot candidate set = ACTIVE STUDENT-role users via a new Identity `CandidateDirectory` contract; AccessGrant-driven population deferred to Commerce (P9). Deferred.
- GAP-058: Frontend — P5 runtime frontend is a lean slice (single `/exam/[attemptId]` runner); richer runtime UX (palette screen, section timer UI, offline queue) deferred. Partial.
- GAP-059: Performance — P5 load tests are in-process concurrency tests, not 5,000-user staging load (extends GAP-038/053). Open.
- GAP-060: Section-wise timer — pilot implements only the global timer; section timers need a schema amendment. Deferred.
- GAP-061: Migrations — Delivery tables are V11/V12/V13, not the recommended V6/V12/V13. Informational.

That's a lot; let me include 055-061 (7 gaps → total 61). Reasonable and honest.

73	| GAP-051 | Blueprint validation | Blueprint `language.languageIds` are not verified against the Academic Language catalog at publication | An unknown language id could be configured in a published blueprint | `13-P4 §83` lists `verifyLanguage` as a required Academic contract operation | Verify language ids at blueprint publication | Open |

77	| GAP-055 | Migrations | `background_job` (Infrastructure-owned) is created in `V12` ahead of its phase | Delivery inherits an already-created table | The evaluation handoff (`14-P5 §67`) has no other durable mechanism | Infrastructure phase adopts it | By-design (ADR-0018) |
78	| GAP-056 | Authorization | The frozen Identity catalogue defines no Delivery permission codes | Minimal operational codes (`DELIVERY_PREPARE_ATTEMPTS`, `DELIVERY_ATTEMPT_VIEW_ANY`, `DELIVERY_OPERATE`) are seeded | `14-P5 §83` says codes are not invented in Delivery | Realign with the Identity catalogue (with GAP-021/036/048) | Open |
79	| GAP-057 | Eligibility | The pilot candidate population is the ACTIVE `STUDENT`-role operational set via a new Identity `CandidateDirectory` contract | `AccessGrant`-driven population is not yet implemented | `14-P5 §19` makes this a documented P5 design decision; Commerce is P9 | Adopt `AccessGrant` population when Commerce lands | Deferred |
80	| GAP-058 | Frontend | The P5 runtime frontend is a lean slice: one `/exam/[attemptId]` runner with autosave, ETag handling, palette state, timer, and submit | Richer runtime UX (multi-tab guard, offline queue, section-timer UI, dedicated palette screen) is not built | Scope trimming for this slice | Complete runtime UX in a follow-up | Partial / Open |
81	| GAP-059 | Performance | P5 verifies start-burst race safety with in-process concurrency tests, not the 5,000-user staging load (`§106-§113`) | No p95/p99, DB CPU, connection, lock-wait, or WAL evidence | Requires staged data and a load harness | Performance phase / staging (extends GAP-038/053) | Open |
82	| GAP-060 | Timer | The pilot implements only the global timer (`started_on` + duration) | Section-wise timing needs new persisted state | `14-P5 §58` forbids silently adding a timer table | Schema amendment before enabling | Deferred |
83	| GAP-061 | Migrations | Delivery is `V11`/`V12`/`V13`, not the recommended `V6`/`V12`/`V13` | Numbering differs from the recommended list | Earlier phases used `V2`-`V10`; the schema document says numbering may differ | None (informational) | Resolved / informational |

  • V11__create_delivery_runtime_tables.sql — student_attempt, assessment_session, attempt_question, attempt_question_option, student_response (exact frozen columns, UNIQUE(attempt_id, question_version_id), UNIQUE(attempt_id, display_order), option uniques, CHECK-constrained statuses, all named indexes).
  • V12__create_background_job.sql — frozen Infrastructure table, prerequisite for the evaluation handoff (GAP-055).
  • V13__seed_delivery_master_data.sql — the 3 operational Delivery permission codes (GAP-056).

Endpoints added (the exact frozen 14)

  • POST /assessment-schedules/{id}/prepare-attempts (202, with a durable jobId), POST …/start
  • GET /sessions/active, GET /sessions/{id}, POST /sessions/{id}/recover, POST /sessions/{id}/end
  • GET /attempts, GET /attempts/{id}, GET /attempts/{id}/questions, GET …/questions/{questionVersionId}
  • GET/PUT /attempts/{id}/responses/{questionVersionId} (ETag + If-Match, Cache-Control: no-store), GET …/responses
  • POST /attempts/{id}/submit

Backend design — Delivery module (api/application/domain/repository/dto/engine/worker/observability/exception) with cross-module contracts only: AssessmentDefinitionCatalog (published P4 handoff), extended ContentCatalog.runtimeQuestion (student-safe content), new Identity CandidateDirectory, and the Delivery-owned policy catalog. No cross-module repository access.

The four verifications

  • Start Exam performs zero snapshot inserts — attempt_question/attempt_question_option counts identical before/after; repeated Start is idempotent; 16-thread burst on one attempt and 10 concurrent users insert nothing (StartExamIntegrationTest, StartBurstConcurrencyTest).
  • Stale-write precondition — first save 200 + ETag; matching If-Match 200 + new ETag; stale If-Match → 412 STALE_RESPONSE with the newer value preserved; If-Match: * with no row → 412; same pair updates one row.
  • Recovery returns the same snapshot — identical question-version list, started_on unchanged, no second session.
  • Concurrent-user start burst — 16 concurrent starts → 1 logical start, 1 session, 0 snapshot inserts; 10 users → 10 distinct attempts, 1 session each.

Plus submission idempotency (one SUBMITTED, one EVALUATION job, no double session end) and no correct-answer leakage.

Tests added — backend 19 (migration 5, start/security 5, precondition 3, recovery/submission 3, burst 2, metrics 1); frontend 9 (schema 5, runtime helpers 4).

Commands run

  • backend && ./mvnw.cmd test → 184 tests, 0 failures, BUILD SUCCESS (170 + 14 split across two foreground runs because the suite exceeds the 10-minute foreground cap).
  • frontend: npm run type-check PASS · npm run lint PASS · npm test → 56 tests, 18 files, PASS · npm run build PASS (/exam/[attemptId] built).

Docs — docs/testing/p5/README.md (evidence), ADR-0018 (+ index), docs/known-gaps.md → 61 entries (GAP-055 background_job ahead of Infrastructure, 056 Delivery permission candidates, 057 pilot candidate population pending Commerce/AccessGrant, 058 lean runtime frontend, 059 in-process bursts not a 5,000-user staging load, 060 global-only timer, 061 migration numbering), docs/implementation-status.md updated to P5.

1: # Known Gaps and Deferred Items
2: 
3: - **Version:** 1.5
4: - **Updated:** 2026-10-10
5: - **Applies to:** P0 (Foundation) and P1 (Identity and Security)
6: 
7: ## Purpose
8: 
9: `AGENTS.md` rule 20 requires that a frozen-contract gap is **stopped, reported, and not silently
10: resolved**. This register is the single durable place where those gaps and deliberate deferrals
11: are recorded, so they are visible to review, QA, and the next phase rather than buried in chat or
12: in a passing comment.
13: 
14: A gap here is not automatically a defect. Each entry states whether it is an **open gap**, a
15: **deferred item with a target phase**, a **by-design limitation**, or an **information note**. No
16: entry authorises a change to a frozen contract; a change that would do so must go through the
17: architecture-change process (`specs/09-P0-Foundation-V1.5.md §86`).

24: | GAP-002 | Password reset | No delivery channel (email/SMS) for reset tokens | End users cannot receive a reset link through the product yet; the backend reset contract is complete | Email/notification platform is out of P1 scope (`10-P1 §184`) | P10 Communication | Deferred (ADR-0012) |
25: | GAP-003 | Authentication | Refresh is bound to the current active session and cannot revive an expired access token | A user whose access token expired must log in again rather than silently refresh | No `refresh_token`/`token_hash`/`expires_on` column exists and `§42` forbids adding one | Revisit if a true refresh token is required (schema change + ADR) | By-design limitation (ADR-0011) |
26: | GAP-004 | Sessions | No idle/inactivity timeout; only an absolute lifetime | A session stays valid until its absolute lifetime even if unused | `login_session` has no last-activity column (`10-P1 §41, §42`) | Revisit with a schema change or ADR | By-design limitation (ADR-0011) |
27: | GAP-005 | Frontend token storage | Access token in `sessionStorage` is readable by page scripts | XSS on the origin can exfiltrate the token; mitigated by short TTL + immediate session revocation but not eliminated | Source does not prescribe storage; P1 chose one documented strategy (`10-P1 §99`; ADR-0010) | Hardening phase (httpOnly cookie requires an API transport change) | Open (accepted for P1) |

| GAP-062 | Timer enforcement | The server derives and returns the deadline, but protected runtime mutations are not rejected after the deadline, and auto-submit on expiry is not implemented | An expired attempt is only finalized when the student submits; late response saves are not blocked server-side | `14-P5 §56` requires non-submission state-changing actions to be rejected after the deadline | Add deadline enforcement to protected mutations and the expiry auto-submit path | Open |

| GAP-063 | Navigation | Navigation policy is resolved and returned, and the UI honours it, but the frozen contract exposes no navigation endpoint | Section/question transitions are not server-round-tripped; the backend cannot reject an out-of-policy transition | `05-Contract §62` defines no navigation route; `14-P5 §36` describes validation without a frozen endpoint | Add only with contract review | By-design / open |

| GAP-064 | Authorization | `DELIVERY_ATTEMPT_VIEW_ANY` is seeded but no staff cross-user read path is exposed; `GET /attempts/{id}` and the question/response reads are self-scoped only | Staff/operations cannot read another student's runtime attempt through the API yet | `14-P5 §83` marks staff access "according to permission"; no staff read route is frozen | Implement the permission-gated staff read when required | Open |

| GAP-065 | Session lifecycle | Assessment-session expiry/logout is not swept by a worker and attempt-vs-session expiry are not separated | A session ends only on submit or an explicit `/sessions/{id}/end`; a stale session remains ACTIVE indefinitely | `14-P5 §90, §91` describe a session expiry worker; the frozen schema has no session expiry column | Add a derived-expiry sweep (like the login-session job) | Open |

| GAP-066 | Idempotency | `Idempotency-Key` is neither required nor stored for submit; idempotency relies on the conditional `IN_PROGRESS -> SUBMITTED` transition and the `EVALUATION` job lookup | Replaying a submit is safe for state, but there is no request-level dedupe record | `05-Contract §17/§18` mention `Idempotency-Key`; `14-P5 §47` leaves the mechanism to implementation | Add request-level dedupe if a non-idempotent effect is introduced | By-design / open |

| GAP-067 | Listing | `GET /attempts` filters, sorts, and paginates in memory over the caller's attempts rather than a database-side page query | Acceptable for a single student's history; not suitable for large staff-scoped listing | `14-P5 §84` requires indexed, projectional queries; the pilot's per-user set is small | Convert to a database-paged projection when staff listing is needed | Open |

| GAP-068 | Delivery policy | Delivery policies are resolved at runtime but there is no authoring API; only the seeded `DEFAULT` policy exists | New policies cannot be created through the API | No `delivery_policy` endpoint is frozen (`14-P5 §70`) | Manage policy rows operationally, or add an endpoint with contract review | By-design / informational |

| GAP-069 | Time tracking | `timeSpentSeconds` is client-supplied and only range-bounded server-side; there is no server-measured per-question time | Persisted time is an estimate, not authoritative | `14-P5 §46` calls it a client measurement persisted on save; no timing table is permitted | Accept as an estimate; add analytics validation if needed | By-design / informational |

Good. 8 rows.
